**Report:** A notice described **Mazon**, a Jewish hunger-relief organization, allocating **two $50,000 emergency grants** for survivors of Hurricane Mitch and discussed giving tied to family celebrations.  
**Assessment:** **A reported grant allocation, not independently audited evidence of final transfer, spending, or the total relief funds raised.** The November synagogue-loss account and this December aid notice show separate community consequences of one disaster.

### 1999

**HN-842 — March 12, 1999 — Indianapolis students disagree about Y2K preparation and panic**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 13 — [original student interviews](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR19990312-01.1.13); [GAO post-rollover assessment](https://www.gao.gov/products/t-aimd-00-70).  
**Report:** A student-written feature described people buying bottled water or withdrawing money, alongside young interviewees who saw little reason to panic over the computer date rollover.  
**Assessment:** **Reported personal expectations, not proof that a catastrophic Y2K failure would occur.** GAO later documented **limited actual rollover disruption**, following a large international prevention effort; the outcome neither establishes every earlier warning as reasonable nor shows that preparation was unnecessary.

**HN-843 — April 28, 1999 — Kosovo refugee assistance interpreted through Israeli aid work**  
**Source:** *Jewish Post*, p. 19 — [original opinion column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JPOST19990428-01.1.19).  
**Report:** In an **“Israel: As I See It”** column, **Samson Krupnick** described relief reaching people who had fled **Kosovo** into neighboring Albania and Macedonia and explained why Israeli assistance resonated with the columnist.  
**Assessment:** **Attributed humanitarian commentary, not a neutral history of Balkan ethnic relations.** The essay makes broad claims about regional peoples and their past that are not verified in this pass; the existence of refugees and relief efforts is distinct from accepting those historical generalizations.

**HN-844 — May 20, 1999 — Student newspaper documents Columbine's thirteen victims**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original student reporting](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR19990520.1.3).  
**Report:** Student reporter **Cassie Borges** recounted the **April 20** Columbine High School attack, naming student gunmen **Eric Harris and Dylan Klebold**, reporting **12 students and one teacher killed**, and describing the fear the attack sparked among students elsewhere.  
**Assessment:** **The victim count is distinct from the deaths of the two perpetrators.** The page also speculated about particular categories of intended victims and the perpetrators' thoughts; those interpretations are not established solely by a student writer's reconstruction.

**HN-845 — May 20, 1999 — Lafayette high school considers locked classroom doors after Columbine**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 1 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR19990520.1.1).  
**Report:** A separate front-page student discussion connected Columbine to anxiety at a Lafayette school and described proposals to keep classroom doors closed during lessons and admit only identified visitors.  
**Assessment:** **A contemporaneous local safety discussion, not evidence that a specific door policy was implemented everywhere or would necessarily prevent an attack.** The educational response is historically distinct from reporting on the Colorado shooting itself.

**HN-846 — August 25, 1999 — Turkey earthquake rescue story records twins' different fates**  
**Source:** *Jewish Post*, p. 8 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JPOST19990825-01.1.8).  
**Report:** As Israeli responders helped in northwest Turkey, the paper said nine-year-old **Shiron Franco** was found alive after more than **98 hours** beneath rubble, while his twin brother **Arieh** had died; information about other relatives and missing tourists was still developing.  
**Assessment:** **A specific rescue report, not the final casualty roster of the August 17 İzmit earthquake.** The estimated numbers of trapped or missing people in this article must not be recast as verified final fatalities.

**HN-847 — September 24, 1999 — East Timor students report violence following independence referendum**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 4 — [original student reporting](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR19990924.1.4); [U.S. State Department rights review](https://1997-2001.state.gov/global/human_rights/1999_hrp_report/overview.html).  
**Report:** The article described mass displacement and killings after East Timor's overwhelming vote for independence, giving a preliminary **over-30,000 refugees** figure and describing violence linked to Indonesian forces. It dated the vote **August 31**.  
**Assessment:** **The U.N.-sponsored ballot took place August 30, not August 31.** The State Department later documented severe violence by militias supported or controlled by Indonesian military elements; the article's displaced-person estimate was an early snapshot, not a total for the crisis.

**HN-848 — December 24, 1999 — Indianapolis hospital publicizes Y2K readiness before rollover**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 18 — [original notice](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR19991224-01.1.18); [GAO review](https://www.gao.gov/products/t-aimd-00-70).  
**Report:** St. Vincent Hospital printed a **September 1999 readiness disclosure** describing tests and spending meant to keep patient care uninterrupted as two-digit date systems changed to 2000.  
**Assessment:** **A hospital's stated preparations and assurance, not an audited statement that every system passed later tests.** GAO's national finding of limited disruptions occurred after widespread remediation; this notice is evidence of preparation as well as public anxiety.

### 2000

**HN-849 — April 20, 2000 — Muncie editorial puts Elián González's father ahead of election politics**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 2 — [original editorial](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20000420-01.1.2).  
**Report:** The editorial argued that **six-year-old Elián González** should be returned to his father in Cuba and criticized Democratic and Republican politicians for using the boy's custody dispute to court voters.  
**Assessment:** **An attributed custody and political opinion, not a court judgment.** Later immigration proceedings, appeals, and the child's return followed their own legal path; the column should not be confused with a contemporaneous order deciding every question.

**HN-850 — November 10, 2000 — Black newspaper reports Florida voters' election complaints**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 1 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20001110-01.1.1); [National Archives certified electoral result](https://www.archives.gov/electoral-college/2000).  
**Report:** A syndicated report from the **Westside Gazette** described voters in **Miami-Dade, Broward, and Palm Beach counties** raising allegations of unfair treatment as George W. Bush's apparent lead in Florida proved tiny and an automatic recount began.  
**Assessment:** **Complaints and fears are not adjudicated evidence of every alleged irregularity.** National Archives records show Bush ultimately received Florida's electoral votes, despite **Al Gore winning the national popular vote**; the official outcome does not, by itself, resolve each voter's reported experience.

**HN-851 — November 21, 2000 — High-school paper tracks networks' withdrawn Florida projections**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 12 — [original student account](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20001121.1.12); [National Archives election history](https://www.archives.gov/publications/prologue/2008/fall/electoral.html).  
**Report:** Students recounted television networks first predicting Florida for **Gore**, then withdrawing that projection, subsequently favoring **Bush**, and a short-lived concession call amid changing totals.  
**Assessment:** **Election-night projections are not certified returns.** The eventual legal and congressional processes determined the electoral-vote outcome, while the article captures genuine uncertainty that post-election retellings can erase.

**HN-852 — December 15, 2000 — Indiana's statehood ceremony unfolds beside contested Florida vote**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 1 — [original AP report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20001215-01.1.1).  
**Report:** At a Statehouse observance of **Indiana's 184th statehood anniversary**, officials joked about Supreme Court justices being particularly busy in Florida while attention remained fixed on that state's contested electoral votes.  
**Assessment:** **A local ceremony with contemporaneous election commentary, not a source for detailed Supreme Court holdings.** Indiana's commemoration and the national litigation are separate news events brought together by the report's timing.

**HN-853 — December 20, 2000 — Nappanee-area Marine helped USS Cole recovery after bombing**  
**Source:** *Nappanee Advance-News*, p. 2 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=NAN20001220.1.2); [FBI case history](https://www.fbi.gov/history/cases-and-criminals/uss-cole-bombing).  
**Report:** The paper profiled **Lance Cpl. John P. Wolf**, a local high-school graduate whose Marine expeditionary unit supported rescue, security, and recovery after the **October 12** bombing of **USS Cole** in Aden. It reported **17 sailors killed**.  
**Assessment:** **A particular servicemember's documented deployment, not a full operational log or judicial account of the attack.** The FBI confirms 17 sailors killed by the explosive-laden boat; responsibilities for rescue, investigation, and eventual prosecution are distinct.

### 2001

**HN-854 — September 14, 2001 — Indianapolis residents speak in the first aftermath of 9/11**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 1 — [original front page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20010914-01.1.1).  
**Report:** The Black community newspaper described shock and prayer after hijacked airplanes struck the **World Trade Center** and **Pentagon**, quoting local resident **Joe Atkins** and reporting death counts still developing.  
**Assessment:** **Contemporaneous public reaction, not a final victim register or completed inquiry.** The September 11 attacks unfolded on September **11**; the Friday publication captures how local people processed information in the immediate days after them.

**HN-855 — September 14, 2001 — A sister report describes interrupted work and relatives feared missing**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 6 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20010914-01.1.6).  
**Report:** Indianapolis workers and parents described the loss of normal safety, inability to concentrate at offices, worries about loved ones around Manhattan, and attempts to console children after televised reports of the attacks.  
**Assessment:** **First-person emotion and uncertainty, not evidence that any particular reported missing relative died.** This city-level response extends the front-page article; no named person's fate should be inferred from a fear expressed that week.

**HN-856 — September 20, 2001 — Indiana investigator remembers seeing 9/11 unfold on television**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 39 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20010920-01.1.39).  
**Report:** A profile followed a former investigator connected with Indiana as he watched a second aircraft strike the World Trade Center and feared additional attacks while news from Washington and Pennsylvania remained incomplete.  
**Assessment:** **An eyewitness account of watching live broadcasts, not firsthand observation from an attack site or an attribution of criminal responsibility.** The sequence of reports illustrates uncertainty; the later federal inquiry supplies a different evidentiary level.

**HN-857 — October 12, 2001 — Congressional Black Caucus chair urges broader aims in Afghanistan**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 7 — [original report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20011012-01.1.7).  
**Report:** At the beginning of U.S. military strikes in Afghanistan, the paper quoted **Rep. Eddie Bernice Johnson**, then chair of the Congressional Black Caucus, warning that armed force alone could not resolve the international conflict and urging listening and diplomacy.  
**Assessment:** **A named legislator's stated position, not evidence that the subsequent war followed or rejected every proposed approach.** It records dissent and complexity during an overwhelmingly urgent post-9/11 news cycle.

**HN-858 — October 18, 2001 — Black newspaper insists the enemy is terrorists, not Muslims**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 2 — [original editorial](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20011018-01.1.2).  
**Report:** An editorial titled **“Wage war against terrorists, not Muslims”** discussed British and American bombing of Afghanistan and drew a firm distinction between militant perpetrators and religion as a whole.  
**Assessment:** **A public anti-scapegoating argument and interpretation of military statements, not an official assessment of targets struck or combat casualties.** The distinction between specific offenders and religious communities is central to the historical source.

**HN-859 — November 1, 2001 — Anthrax editorial alleges unequal official attention**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 2 — [original editorial](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20011101-01.1.2); [CDC retrospective epidemiology](https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/eid/article/8/10/02-0353_article).  
**Report:** The paper criticized perceived disparities in how suspected and confirmed **anthrax exposures in Washington** were addressed amid widespread fears over mailed spores.  
**Assessment:** **An editorial allegation about fairness, not an audited interagency comparison.** CDC subsequently identified **22 cases and five deaths** in the 2001 attacks; epidemiological confirmation of the cases is not independent confirmation of each charge of unequal treatment.

**HN-860 — November 20, 2001 — School newspaper traces anthrax scare through early cases**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 7 — [original student report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20011120.1.7); [CDC findings](https://stacks.cdc.gov/view/cdc/3107); [FBI investigation history](https://www.fbi.gov/history/cases-and-criminals/amerithrax-or-anthrax-investigation).  
**Report:** A feature called **“Anthrax hits home”** tracked the first Florida illnesses, subsequent concerns at broadcast-news offices, and letters addressed to national politicians, with dates and early medical details from unfolding coverage.  
**Assessment:** **A developing account, not the final case series or a proven identification of the sender.** CDC's final outbreak description specifies 22 cases and five deaths; the FBI later attributed the letters to Bruce Ivins, **who died before being charged or tried**, so official investigative conclusions must not be called a criminal conviction.

### 2002

**HN-861 — January 11, 2002 — Olympic preview questions Salt Lake security and hosting costs**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 20 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20020111-01.1.20).  
**Report:** In the approach to the **Salt Lake City Winter Olympics**, the paper compared expenditure figures with previous Games, described increased security and technology costs, and discussed the publicity surrounding torch travel and Aretha Franklin's Olympic song.  
**Assessment:** **Pre-Games budget and promotional reporting, not a final accounting of public cost, benefit, or security effectiveness.** Its comparison categories and quoted figures need original organizing-committee budgets for rigorous validation.

**HN-862 — August 22, 2002 — WorldCom bankruptcy becomes an argument about the economy**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 10 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20020822-01.1.10).  
**Report:** A political report quoted **Jesse Jackson** connecting corporate scandals and **WorldCom's bankruptcy** with skepticism about economic stewardship before the next presidential election.  
**Assessment:** **A political figure's argument, not a financial investigation determining every allegation about WorldCom or other companies.** Corporate filings, prosecutions, and accounting records are required to assign responsibility; the newspaper documents how the scandal entered broader political rhetoric.

**HN-863 — September 5, 2002 — Ball State professor warns invasion of Iraq could be costly**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 28 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20020905-01.1.28).  
**Report:** Former network reporter and Ball State professor **Phil Bremen**, drawing on experience of the Iran–Iraq War, cautioned that Iraqi civilians might resist a U.S. effort to overthrow **Saddam Hussein** and that war could carry serious human and political costs.  
**Assessment:** **An expert's contemporary forecast, not proof of what Iraqi populations would eventually do.** The United States had **not yet invaded Iraq**; later events can be compared with this prediction without portraying it as foreknowledge.

**HN-864 — October 23, 2002 — Student report captures sniper fear just before the arrests**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20021023.1.3); [FBI case account](https://www.fbi.gov/history/cases-and-criminals/dc-snipers).  
**Report:** Under **“Sniper shootings paralyze Washington,”** the student reporter described people avoiding routine activities, a **$500,000** reward, and **nine dead and two wounded** at the moment the article was prepared. The perpetrators were then described as unknown.  
**Assessment:** **An early count and unsolved-case snapshot, not a completed series or suspect identification.** Authorities arrested **John Allen Muhammad and Lee Boyd Malvo on October 24**; their eventual convictions and full victim tallies belong to a later record.

**HN-865 — November 1, 2002 — Muslim community rejects religious blame for sniper shootings**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 13 — [original community statement](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20021101-01.1.13).  
**Report:** A published statement offered condolences to families affected by the Washington-area sniper killings and objected to blaming **Islam or ordinary Muslim residents** for the acts of specific suspects.  
**Assessment:** **A community moral position, not evidence about the convicted offenders' motives or a case-wide defense statement.** A suspect's professed identity and a religion's adherents are not interchangeable; the article records how the community responded to collective suspicion.

**HN-866 — November 25, 2002 — High-school students debate Iraq's alleged unconventional weapons**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original student reporting](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20021125.1.3); [Senate prewar-intelligence review](https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/2004/07/09/publications-report-us-intelligence-communitys-prewar-intelligence-assessments-iraq-s-2386-july-9/).  
**Report:** Students and an Army officer weighed whether the United States should attack Iraq on **reports and fears that it might possess weapons of mass destruction**, with interviewees disagreeing about military intervention.  
**Assessment:** **Claims presented in a prewar debate, not proof that the asserted stockpiles existed.** Later official investigations questioned major prewar intelligence assessments; neither the students' hopes nor their fears should be rewritten as confirmed 2002 knowledge.

### 2003

**HN-867 — February 7, 2003 — Black press memorializes Columbia astronaut Michael Anderson**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 1 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20030207-01.1.1); [NASA investigation history](https://ntrs.nasa.gov/citations/20070017902).  
**Report:** The paper featured **Lt. Col. Michael P. Anderson**, one of **seven astronauts** killed when **Space Shuttle Columbia** broke apart during reentry on February **1**, emphasizing his place among African American astronauts.  
**Assessment:** **An individual remembrance and community perspective, not technical evidence about the vehicle's loss.** The engineering causes were still under investigation when this article appeared.

**HN-868 — February 12, 2003 — Student shuttle article speculates about payload weight**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 1 — [original report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20030212.1.1); [Columbia Accident Investigation Board report](https://ntrs.nasa.gov/archive/nasa/casi.ntrs.nasa.gov/20030066167.pdf).  
**Report:** A student investigation summary described Columbia at high altitude, its return expected within minutes, and raised the possibility that carrying many experiments meant the shuttle's payload might have been too heavy.  
**Assessment:** **The payload conjecture is not the established accident cause.** The later Columbia board traced fatal thermal-protection damage to **foam striking the left-wing leading edge during launch**, followed by hot-gas penetration at reentry; it also identified institutional safety failures.

**HN-869 — March 28, 2003 — Black newspaper pictures air operations during Iraq's second war week**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 1 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20030328-01.1.1).  
**Report:** In an article on the invasion entering its second week, the front page showed a launch crew on **USS Theodore Roosevelt** preparing an electronic-warfare aircraft for a flight over northern Iraq.  
**Assessment:** **A specific wire photograph and contemporary operational description, not a tally of bombing effects or a postwar legality assessment.** The image supports the presence of operations, not an inference about all targets or civilian casualties.

**HN-870 — May 30, 2003 — Columnist asks where Iraq's alleged weapons are**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 9 — [original opinion page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20030530-01.1.9); [Senate intelligence inquiry](https://www.intelligence.senate.gov/2004/07/09/publications-report-us-intelligence-communitys-prewar-intelligence-assessments-iraq-s-2386-july-9/).  
**Report:** **Bill Fletcher Jr.** questioned why the U.S. government had not produced the much-discussed weapons of mass destruction after invading Iraq, challenging prewar claims of imminent danger.  
**Assessment:** **A contemporary critique, not a completed inspector's report in May 2003.** Subsequent intelligence and Iraq Survey Group analyses showed major failures in prewar claims; the column records an early, clearly labeled question before those investigations finished.

**HN-871 — August 20, 2003 — Reader links Europe's heat emergency to North American blackout**  
**Source:** *Jewish Post*, p. 23 — [original letter](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JPOST20030820-01.1.23); [WHO retrospective heat-mortality summary](https://www.who.int/en/news-room/fact-sheets/detail/climate-change-heat-and-health).  
**Report:** A letter called the European summer heat wave and the huge U.S.–Canadian blackout a **“wake-up call”** for religious communities to place environmental responsibility at the center of their work.  
**Assessment:** **An environmental appeal, not a technical finding that climate change caused the electric grid failure.** WHO describes roughly **70,000 heat-related deaths** in Europe's 2003 heat emergency; the North American outage had its own engineering investigation and cause.

**HN-872 — August 22, 2003 — Blackout editorial lists several competing explanations**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 11 — [original opinion page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20030822-01.1.11); [U.S. Department of Energy blackout account](https://www.energy.gov/oe/august-2003-blackout).  
**Report:** After the **August 14** outage across the Northeast and Ontario, an opinion writer weighed proposed causes including aging infrastructure, demand, and deregulation, with **50 million people** reported affected.  
**Assessment:** **These were explanations under discussion, not all proven causes of the outage.** The joint U.S.–Canada task force investigated a sequence of technical and operational failures; the article should be read as a period debate rather than the final causal report.

**HN-873 — September 24, 2003 — Student reporting reconstructs the blackout's early cascade**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 2 — [original student report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20030924.1.2); [Energy Department overview](https://www.energy.gov/oe/august-2003-blackout).  
**Report:** A student feature described operators at a New York power-system control center trying to understand abnormal flows before the August 14 outage spread, including accounts of stranded commuters sleeping outdoors.  
**Assessment:** **A narrated reconstruction with human consequences, not a substitute for the final multi-jurisdiction grid-event chronology.** The report's particular flow figures and sequencing require comparison with dispatcher logs before technical attribution.

**HN-874 — November 14, 2003 — SARS response used to argue for international disease cooperation**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 18 — [original AP report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20031114-01.1.18); [WHO history of SARS](https://ojs.wpro.who.int/ojs/index.php/wpsar/article/download/208/295?inline=1).  
**Report:** From a medical and policy meeting in Beijing, the newspaper described cooperation against **SARS and AIDS** and quoted former President **Bill Clinton** on infections crossing borders quickly.  
**Assessment:** **Reported international health diplomacy, not proof that every policy discussed was implemented.** WHO's historical summary records approximately **8,096 SARS cases and 774 deaths** in the 2002–2003 outbreak; the pandemic-risk analogy and the measured epidemic are separate claims.

### 2004

**HN-875 — March 5, 2004 — Oregon officials begin issuing same-sex marriage licenses**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 15 — [original AP report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20040305-01.1.15).  
**Report:** A report from Portland said county officials were issuing marriage licenses to same-sex couples as legal and political arguments unfolded in **Oregon, California, and New York**.  
**Assessment:** **Licenses being issued at a particular point do not by themselves establish that the marriages were subsequently recognized as legally valid.** State court decisions and later legislation must be checked jurisdiction by jurisdiction.

**HN-876 — March 26, 2004 — Student paper interrogates Newsom's San Francisco marriage policy**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original student feature](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20040326.1.3); [California legislative history](https://leginfo.ca.gov/pub/05-06/bill/asm/ab_0001-0050/ab_19_cfa_20050425_134139_asm_comm.html).  
**Report:** Student writer **Alex Nelson** described Mayor **Gavin Newsom** authorizing same-sex marriage licenses and presented conflicting arguments about equal treatment and established marriage traditions.  
**Assessment:** **A snapshot of a legal dispute, not a final constitutional judgment.** California's high court halted the issuance and later invalidated the **4,037** San Francisco licenses issued in 2004; broader marriage-rights law continued evolving afterward.

**HN-877 — June 4, 2004 — Abu Ghraib photographs spark reflection on U.S. ideals and conduct**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 9 — [original commentary](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20040604-01.1.9).  
**Report:** A columnist contrasted American rhetoric about liberty with evidence of prisoners' abuse at **Abu Ghraib** in Iraq and questioned what national ideals meant in practice.  
**Assessment:** **A critical moral argument responding to widely published photographs, not a complete account of every incident or individual perpetrator.** Findings about command responsibility, criminal liability, and operational policy require military investigative and court records beyond this page.

**HN-878 — June 9, 2004 — Newspaper describes FBI's false Madrid fingerprint identification**  
**Source:** *Jewish Post*, p. 11 — [original commentary](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JPOST20040609-01.1.11); [Justice Department inspector-general investigation](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/s0603/index.htm).  
**Report:** The commentary described American lawyer **Brandon Mayfield** being arrested after FBI fingerprint examiners said his print matched evidence tied to the **March 11 Madrid train bombings**, then released when Spanish officials attributed the print to another man. The writer also debated whether religious profiling shaped the case.  
**Assessment:** **The erroneous fingerprint identification is firmly documented by the DOJ inspector general.** Claims about the precise relative influence of religion, examiner methodology, and investigative judgment must be tied to the actual review, not decided merely by the commentary's politics. Mayfield was **not** a proven Madrid bomber.

**HN-879 — June 18, 2004 — Indiana civil-liberties commentary connects Iraqi prisoner abuse and domestic detention**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 11 — [original opinion page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20040618-01.1.11).  
**Report:** A civil-liberties writer invoked Abu Ghraib photographs, post-9/11 detention and deportation of Muslim men, and incidents involving **Evansville residents** to argue for constitutional accountability.  
**Assessment:** **Claims within an advocacy essay, not fully adjudicated individual detention records.** The article documents a contemporary rights campaign; each accusation about a named agency, arrest, or particular detainee needs case-specific evidence.

**HN-880 — September 15, 2004 — Beslan school massacre triggers a dispute over language in headlines**  
**Source:** *Jewish Post*, p. 35 — [original media-criticism column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JPOST20040915-01.1.35); [European Court of Human Rights case-law summary](https://ks.echr.coe.int/documents/d/echr-ks/clin_april_2017_eng).  
**Report:** A columnist condemned perceived journalistic euphemisms for the gunmen in the **Beslan, Russia school siege**, stating that **some 400 people** had died and comparing words used by international newsrooms.  
**Assessment:** **A polemic about terminology and an approximate early casualty figure, not a final victim register or impartial media study.** The European Court's later case summary describes **approximately 334 civilian deaths, including 186 children**; detailed responsibility and the state's response remained subjects of legal review.

**HN-881 — December 31, 2004 — First Indian Ocean tsunami reports give a toll above 50,000**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 6 — [original commentary](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20041231-01.1.6); [NOAA historical tsunami figures](https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/products/natural-hazards/tsunamis-earthquakes-volcanoes/tsunamis/global-historical-data).  
**Report:** A reflection on the recent **December 26** Indian Ocean earthquake and tsunami said **more than 50,000** had died and warned that contaminated water could complicate relief.  
**Assessment:** **An extremely early partial toll, not the full catastrophe.** NOAA's later global historical dataset attributes approximately **227,899 deaths** to the earthquake and tsunami, a differently dated and much more complete estimate. The source is evidence of how incomplete the picture still was that week.

### 2005

**HN-882 — February 15, 2005 — High-school coverage revises tsunami magnitude of human loss**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original student report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20050215.1.3); [NOAA historical database](https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/products/natural-hazards/tsunamis-earthquakes-volcanoes/tsunamis/global-historical-data).  
**Report:** A student feature on rebuilding after the Indian Ocean tsunami reported **more than 220,000 deaths** and described relief and reconstruction across several countries.  
**Assessment:** **An updated early-2005 estimate, sharply higher than the preceding December's >50,000 count, and close to NOAA's later roughly 227,899.** The figure and geographic reach differ because the news was no longer a first-week snapshot; pledges of aid are not proof that funds were disbursed.

**HN-883 — July 27, 2005 — Rabbi recounts relative's survival of London transit attacks**  
**Source:** *Jewish Post*, p. 19 — [original first-person family account](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JPOST20050727-01.1.19); [British government's official account](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/report-of-the-official-account-of-the-bombings-in-london-on-7th-july-2005).  
**Report:** Rabbi **Shea Hecht** wrote of trouble reaching family by telephone after the **July 7** bombings and retold his brother-in-law **Rabbi Aryeh Sufrin's** experience on a London Underground train affected by the attacks.  
**Assessment:** **A secondhand family-survivor narrative, not a list of all four attackers, trains, or victims.** British official records establish **52 victims killed**, excluding the bombers; the religious interpretation of survival is the writer's own.

**HN-884 — September 9, 2005 — Katrina front page shows evacuees in immediate need of basics**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 1 — [original front page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20050909-01.1.1); [NOAA tropical cyclone report](https://www.nhc.noaa.gov/data/tcr/AL122005_Katrina.pdf).  
**Report:** With the headline **“Hell and High Water,”** the *Recorder* pictured **Cabrini Cooper** holding her infant daughter at a shelter in Florida, reporting that hundreds of New Orleans evacuees were receiving their first clean clothes, meals, and showers in days.  
**Assessment:** **A photographic and named human story, not a regional survey of survivors' conditions.** The tragedy followed Katrina's August **29** Gulf Coast landfall and catastrophic flooding; later official casualty and infrastructure investigations are separate from this immediate relief scene.

**HN-885 — September 9, 2005 — Delayed Katrina response becomes the subject of political anger**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 2 — [original commentary](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20050909-01.1.2); [GAO later preparedness-review overview](https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-09-59r).  
**Report:** A writer denounced what was presented as days of delay by federal agencies in sending troops and supplies to **New Orleans**, comparing the response with national urgency after September 11. The page suggested conditions improved swiftly after military arrivals.  
**Assessment:** **A forceful contemporary account and political judgment, not a verified hour-by-hour response log.** Later government reviews documented multiple failures and strengths in preparedness and response; the article's timing and 'order restored' statements need operational corroboration.

**HN-886 — September 15, 2005 — Former Muncie resident recounts Katrina evacuation**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 1 — [original firsthand narrative](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20050915-01.1.1).  
**Report:** Former Muncie resident **Runako Tsitsi Kumbula** described her experience in New Orleans as Hurricane Katrina approached, including the frightening effort to leave and later confrontation with the storm's damage.  
**Assessment:** **A survivor's own perspective rather than a statistical sample of evacuees.** It deserves preservation in her voice without extrapolating one route or outcome to the entire displaced population.

**HN-887 — September 30, 2005 — Katrina's damage still listed as $63 billion with higher projections**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 1 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20050930-01.1.1).  
**Report:** A hurricane-cost graphic placed **Katrina at $63 billion**, with a parenthetical warning that estimates could climb to **$200 billion**, while discussing other Gulf storms including **Rita**. The graphic mixed historical storms and monetary values.  
**Assessment:** **Provisional storm-loss projections, not comparable settled figures.** Realized insured loss, overall economic harm, inflation-adjusted cost, and different geographic/time boundaries must be distinguished before ranking hurricanes; the unusually wide 2005 range illustrates ongoing uncertainty.

**HN-888 — October 6, 2005 — Muncie holds a resource fair for relocated Katrina families**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 10 — [original local report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20051006-01.1.10).  
**Report:** The paper reported a **Horizon Convention Center** resource fair organized with United Way to help **at least 54 displaced families known to be living in Muncie** find work, housing, and support.  
**Assessment:** **A local service-provider count at one date, not a census of all Gulf Coast evacuees or all families in Indiana.** It documents a concrete secondary migration and civic response weeks after the storm.

**HN-889 — October 26, 2005 — Pakistan earthquake article shows a rapidly rising death toll**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original student report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20051026.1.3); [USGS scientific event record](https://earthquake.usgs.gov/earthquakes/eventpage/usp000e12e).  
**Report:** Student writer **Amy Greer** described the **October 8** Kashmir earthquake, reporting an earlier estimate near **20,000 deaths** had already risen to about **35,000** as buildings collapsed and survivors were rescued.  
**Assessment:** **Interim fatality counts, not a certified final register.** USGS identifies the main earthquake as magnitude **7.6**; exact totals differ across official reporting agencies and geographic definitions, so a final number should not be inferred from this article.

**HN-890 — October 28, 2005 — Indianapolis bird-flu preparedness becomes front-page news**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 1 — [original front page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20051028-01.1.1); [CDC's contemporary influenza update](https://www.cdc.gov/mmwr/preview/mmwrhtml/mm5449a2.htm).  
**Report:** A local feature asked whether Indianapolis could respond if **H5N1 avian influenza** began spreading widely among people, with many citizens doubting institutional preparedness after Katrina.  
**Assessment:** **A plausible preparedness question, not evidence that an H5N1 human pandemic was occurring in Indiana.** The CDC reported **no identified human H5N1 cases in the United States** in its late-2005 assessment and no demonstrated sustained person-to-person transmission.

**HN-891 — October 28, 2005 — Rosa Parks's death prompts reflection on the choice to resist**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 5 — [original memorial essay](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20051028-01.1.5); [Library of Congress Parks timeline](https://www.loc.gov/collections/rosa-parks-papers/articles-and-essays/timeline/).  
**Report:** A remembrance considered the consequences had **Rosa Parks** complied with Montgomery bus segregation in **1955**, treating her refusal and her life of civil-rights activism as a legacy of moral courage.  
**Assessment:** **A counterfactual tribute, not proof that the movement depended on only one person or moment.** Parks died **October 24, 2005**; the Library of Congress holds records documenting her earlier activism, community organizing, and subsequent work beyond the famous bus protest.

**HN-892 — November 4, 2005 — President outlines pandemic vaccine stockpile proposal**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 17 — [original AP dispatch](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20051104-01.1.17); [CDC late-2005 flu surveillance](https://www.cdc.gov/mmwr/preview/mmwrhtml/mm5449a2.htm).  
**Report:** A dispatch said **President George W. Bush** requested funding to prepare a vaccine supply for **20 million Americans** against the then-current bird-flu threat and called for stronger vaccine-production protections.  
**Assessment:** **A proposal and projection about possible future pandemic risk, not evidence that the specified vaccine doses had already been manufactured or that a human H5N1 pandemic had begun.** Separate authorization, procurement, and epidemiology records would be needed to judge implementation.

**HN-893 — December 23, 2005 — Charitable appeals compete after tsunami, hurricanes, and Pakistan quake**  
**Source:** *Indianapolis Recorder*, p. 2 — [original report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=INR20051223-01.1.2).  
**Report:** The newspaper quoted a philanthropy survey reporting, by **December 15**, approximately **$3 billion** in private U.S. hurricane donations, **$1.8 billion** for Indian Ocean tsunami relief, and **$128 million** for the Pakistan earthquake. Interviewees questioned whether what appeared as giving fatigue was instead households reaching their financial limits.  
**Assessment:** **As-of-date donations and respondents' opinions, not audited final receipts or disbursements.** Different disasters' fundraising windows and organizational definitions affect comparison; giving volumes do not alone measure whether aid reached intended beneficiaries.

### 2006

**HN-894 — February 28, 2006 — Sago families receive a false announcement of survival**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 2 — [original student newspaper page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20060228.1.2); [federal mine-investigation report](https://arlweb.msha.gov/fatals/2006/sago/sagoreport.asp).  
**Report:** A student reporter revisited the January 2 Sago mine disaster in West Virginia: relatives briefly celebrated inaccurate news that twelve trapped miners had survived, before learning that only one was alive. The article emphasizes the anguish caused by communication failures.  
**Assessment:** **The false initial rescue news is part of the event's documented public history, not evidence that the eventual toll was concealed.** The Mine Safety and Health Administration's investigation documents **twelve deaths and one survivor**; some physical measurements and timings in the student's retelling are less reliable than the official investigation.

**HN-895 — April 6, 2006 — Civil-rights organizing challenges Katrina recovery inequities**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 21 — [original Judge Greg Mathis column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20060406-01.1.21); [GAO on displaced children and schools](https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-06-680r).  
**Report:** Mathis's column describes a Rainbow/PUSH effort to press the federal government for more equitable help to displaced New Orleans residents, arguing that charitable donations alone could not redress barriers to return and rebuilding.  
**Assessment:** **Advocacy and interpretation**, not an independent audit establishing every asserted government failure. GAO contemporaneously documented significant post-hurricane social-service and education challenges; evaluating each proposed remedy requires subsequent implementation records.

**HN-896 — May 4, 2006 — Children's Defense Fund warns of Katrina's long aftermath**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 9 — [original report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20060504-01.1.9); [GAO examination](https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-06-680r).  
**Report:** A report on the Children's Defense Fund's *Katrina's Children* called for mental-health treatment, mobile health facilities, school-based clinics, and easier Medicaid access for displaced and traumatized children.  
**Assessment:** **A documented advocacy program and unmet-needs warning, not proof every measure was enacted.** Subsequent GAO examinations show why schooling and child welfare posed substantial logistical problems, but intervention outcomes require separate data.

**HN-897 — May 4, 2006 — New Orleans schools rebuild under contested charter arrangements**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 15 — [original Jan Resseger 'Witness for Justice' column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20060504-01.1.15); [GAO review of aid for displaced students](https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-11-839).  
**Report:** Resseger criticized the post-Katrina reorganization of public schools and uncertainty created by school-by-school sponsorship, warning that the replacement of a centralized school system risked leaving vulnerable children without stable support.  
**Assessment:** **A contemporaneous policy critique**, not a neutral measure of charter-school performance. Subsequent aid and enrollment figures need comparison with the article's specific predictions before drawing a causal conclusion.

**HN-898 — August 17, 2006 — Black-press editorial denounces destruction in Lebanon**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 2 — [original editorial](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20060817-01.1.2); [UN Security Council Resolution 1701](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/581053?ln=en).  
**Report:** An editorial condemned civilian deaths and damage during the Israel–Hezbollah fighting and called for urgent international action to end attacks. It blamed multiple actors but clearly advocated a political position.  
**Assessment:** **Editorial assessment, not a final civilian-casualty investigation.** The Security Council adopted Resolution 1701 on **August 11**, calling for cessation of hostilities and an expanded UN role; the newspaper's publication followed the resolution and cannot stand as a full cease-fire compliance assessment.

**HN-899 — November 2, 2006 — Column cites testimony alleging racial vigilantism after Katrina**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 23 — [original 'Witness for Justice' column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20061102-01.1.23).  
**Report:** A religious-social-justice column quotes survivors' accounts gathered by a Katrina justice commission, alleging that armed vigilantes fired at or targeted African American men in the immediate aftermath of the storm.  
**Assessment:** **Serious attributed testimony, not a conviction record or independently established count of assaults.** The underlying witness interviews, police investigations, and any prosecutions must be checked individually; the fact that the claims were printed does not establish each alleged shooting or perpetrator.

**HN-900 — November 6, 2006 — Student column misidentifies North Korea's underground nuclear test**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 4 — [original commentary](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20061106.1.4); [UN Security Council Resolution 1718](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/584729?ln=en).  
**Report:** A columnist called for stronger action against Kim Jong-il after the October North Korean test, but described an underground experiment as the detonation of a **'nuclear missile'**, conflating a nuclear-explosive test with a missile launch.  
**Assessment:** **The terminology in the student commentary is wrong.** The DPRK announced an underground nuclear-weapon test on **October 9, 2006**; the UN condemned that announced test and separately addressed ballistic-missile activity. Its exact yield cannot be inferred from the student's discussion of radiation reports.

### 2007

**HN-901 — January 11, 2007 — Katrina survivors' continuing housing hardship becomes a civil-rights argument**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 26 — [original Judge Greg Mathis column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20070111-01.1.26); [GAO emergency education-aid review](https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-11-839).  
**Report:** Mathis argued that federal housing and rebuilding delays continued to injure displaced Gulf Coast families long after the storm and urged attention to people still struggling to return.  
**Assessment:** **Attributed political commentary**, not a household-level audit. The account is important evidence of dissatisfaction expressed in the Black press; exact assistance failures require administrative and survivor-specific records.

**HN-902 — July 5, 2007 — William Jefferson indicted as his district continues hurricane recovery**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 9 — [original Mathis commentary](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20070705-01.1.9); [DOJ trial outcome](https://www.justice.gov/archives/opa/pr/former-congressman-william-j-jefferson-convicted-bribery-racketeering-money-laundering-and).  
**Report:** The column describes the then-recent **sixteen-count federal indictment** of Louisiana Congressman William Jefferson and argues that his district should not be deprived of Katrina recovery assistance because of its representative's legal troubles.  
**Assessment:** **At publication, the charged conduct was alleged rather than adjudicated.** A federal jury convicted Jefferson on **eleven counts in 2009**. Legal outcomes after that verdict need individual checking; the July 2007 indictment cannot be represented as a contemporaneous conviction.

**HN-903 — July 19, 2007 — NAACP alleges racially discriminatory subprime lending**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 11 — [original civil-rights report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20070719-01.1.11); [DOJ later Countrywide lending investigation](https://www.justice.gov/archives/opa/pr/justice-department-reaches-335-million-settlement-resolve-allegations-lending-discrimination).  
**Report:** The paper says the NAACP sued **fourteen lenders**, alleging African American borrowers faced disproportionately costly subprime loans, including some allegedly qualified for lower-cost products.  
**Assessment:** **A filed lawsuit and statistical allegations, not a verdict against all fourteen defendants.** Later federal investigations and settlements documented discriminatory-pricing and steering allegations involving particular lenders, but these cannot be retroactively treated as judgments on every defendant in the 2007 suit.

**HN-904 — October 25, 2007 — Nappanee eyewitnesses describe a violent nighttime tornado**  
**Source:** *Nappanee Advance-News*, p. 1 — [original local reporting](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=NAN20071025.1.1); [NWS storm assessment](https://www.weather.gov/iwx/20071018_tornado).  
**Report:** Residents recount the late-night **October 18** tornado and sudden damage to homes and a church. The paper prints a police department account of only moments to sound sirens and calls the tornado an 'F3.'  
**Assessment:** **Eyewitness testimony and immediate damage reporting.** Modern NWS documentation rates the tornado **EF3**, with estimated winds near **165 mph** over southeastern Nappanee; the original's older 'F3' wording should not be substituted for the Enhanced Fujita classification.

**HN-905 — November 8, 2007 — Grade-school penny contest raises $3,750.49 for tornado victims**  
**Source:** *Nappanee Advance-News*, p. 6 — [original local fund-raising report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=NAN20071108.1.6); [NWS storm history](https://www.weather.gov/iwx/decade_weather_2000_2009).  
**Report:** Woodview Elementary students turned a grade-versus-grade penny contest into local tornado relief, with the paper reporting **$3,750.49 raised** and recalling their earlier fundraising after Katrina.  
**Assessment:** **Reported receipts for a specific school drive, not proof of how each dollar was subsequently distributed.** The beneficiary was the Nappanee Tornado Victims' Fund; expenditure records remain a separate research question.

**HN-906 — December 6, 2007 — Long-term Nappanee tornado assistance remains available**  
**Source:** *Nappanee Advance-News*, p. 9 — [original service announcement](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=NAN20071206.1.9); [NWS event record](https://www.weather.gov/iwx/20071018_tornado).  
**Report:** A local notice describes the Tornado Victims Assistance Fund, bank and card donation routes, and continued recovery services weeks after the October destruction.  
**Assessment:** **An advertised relief program, not a verified accounting of approvals, awards, or final recovery.** The repeated coverage shows that the local aftermath outlasted the initial national storm headlines.

### 2008

**HN-907 — February 15, 2008 — Central Indiana mortgage-and-investment fraud conviction**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 10 — [original report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20080215-01.1.10).  
**Report:** The paper reports that Indianapolis resident **Jason Keigley** was found guilty in Hendricks County Superior Court of crimes involving an investment and mortgage fraud scheme affecting older residents, citing state and county prosecutors.  
**Assessment:** **A reported court verdict, distinct from the much broader national mortgage crisis.** The original judgment, sentencing, appellate status, victim count, and restitution records have not yet been independently obtained.

**HN-908 — October 16, 2008 — Bailout debate contrasts Wall Street rescue with foreclosures**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 15 — [original Judge Greg Mathis column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20081016-01.1.15); [Federal Reserve crisis retrospective](https://www.federalreservehistory.org/essays/great-recession-and-its-aftermath).  
**Report:** Mathis criticized the newly enacted **$700-billion financial rescue authorization** as insufficient relief for homeowners, while describing actions by state attorneys general against Countrywide mortgage practices.  
**Assessment:** **Policy argument with contemporary lawsuit and settlement reporting.** The $700 billion was a **program authorization**, not a verified lump-sum expenditure or a payment solely to a single institution. The column's broad assessment of homeowner relief must be separated from later program disbursements.

**HN-909 — October 16, 2008 — Black Enterprise frames crash as a retirement-security threat**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 27 — [original syndicated magazine feature](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20081016-01.1.27); [Federal Reserve crisis chronology](https://www.federalreservehistory.org/essays/support-for-specific-institutions).  
**Report:** A *Black Enterprise* retirement-planning feature linked falling retirement portfolios, collapsing financial firms including Lehman Brothers, and the housing bust to a crisis in long-term financial security.  
**Assessment:** **Contemporary consumer-finance framing, not a representative survey of retirement losses.** The Federal Reserve's later history distinguishes Lehman's failure from the support provided to some other firms; the economic categories must not be collapsed into 'all banks were bailed out.'

**HN-910 — November 13, 2008 — Black press republishes Obama's historic victory speech**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 14 — [original speech continuation](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20081113-01.1.14); [National Archives presidential records](https://www.obamalibrary.gov/research/presidential-records).  
**Report:** The newspaper reproduced part of President-elect **Barack Obama's November 4 victory address**, coupling a landmark electoral outcome with his warning that war, financial crisis, and climate danger remained unresolved.  
**Assessment:** **A primary transcript of campaign rhetoric, not evidence that its policy promises were accomplished.** This page begins mid-speech; the complete address and certified election returns are better sources for exact wording and vote totals.

**HN-911 — December 15, 2008 — Zimbabwe cholera toll in the press still climbing**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 6 — [original report continuation](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20081215-01.1.6); [WHO subsequent epidemiological update](https://www.who.int/emergencies/disease-outbreak-news/item/2009_06_09-en).  
**Report:** A feature on Zimbabwe's political and economic collapse says about **18,000 people** had contracted cholera and more than **1,111** had died according to reports circulating by publication.  
**Assessment:** **Mid-outbreak figures, not final totals.** WHO later reported **98,424 suspected cases and 4,276 deaths by May 30, 2009**. The article's unemployment and political characterizations should be attributed to its author rather than presented as independently audited statistics.

### 2009

**HN-912 — March 12, 2009 — Administration explains the frozen-credit cycle**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 9 — [original reprinted presidential-policy text](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20090312-01.1.9); [Federal Reserve crisis history](https://www.federalreservehistory.org/essays/great-recession-and-its-aftermath).  
**Report:** A continued address explains how mortgage losses, banks' fear of lending, and household and business credit constraints compounded unemployment and recession, while describing proposals to stabilize lending.  
**Assessment:** **An administration explanation of policy, not neutral proof of the planned rescue's results.** The excerpt starts on an earlier page; economic outcomes and costs must be checked against subsequent official data.

**HN-913 — May 7, 2009 — H1N1 dispatch reports 403 U.S. cases and one death**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 1 — [original front-page report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20090507-01.1.1); [CDC pandemic burden estimates](https://archive.cdc.gov/www_cdc_gov/h1n1flu/estimates_2009_h1n1.htm).  
**Report:** A dispatch describes international spread of the new swine-origin influenza A(H1N1) and quotes an early CDC tally of **403 U.S. cases and one death**, while expecting more cases and hospitalizations.  
**Assessment:** **An early surveillance snapshot, not the eventual epidemic total.** CDC later estimated approximately **60.8 million U.S. illnesses, 274,304 hospitalizations and 12,469 deaths** for April 2009–April 2010, with ranges reflecting statistical uncertainty.

**HN-914 — September 30, 2009 — Lafayette school takes precautions during H1N1 wave**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, pp. 1 and 3 — [original campus front page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20090930.1.1); [student follow-up](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20090930.1.3); [CDC pandemic history](https://archive.cdc.gov/www_cdc_gov/flu/pandemic-resources/2009-h1n1-pandemic.html).  
**Report:** Students described classroom sanitizers, hand-washing notices, absences and concerns about the emerging H1N1 vaccine; a second article expected first vaccine supplies during October.  
**Assessment:** **Direct local coverage of school response.** Vaccine distribution timing was prospective when written, and H1N1 was a real pandemic; neither panic nor a claim that all students were infected follows from precautions alone.

**HN-915 — November 19, 2009 — Nappanee student absences and vaccinations provide a local flu snapshot**  
**Source:** *Nappanee Advance-News*, p. 1 — [original local report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=NAN20091119.1.1); [CDC's retrospective pandemic estimates](https://archive.cdc.gov/www_cdc_gov/flu/pandemic-resources/2009-h1n1-pandemic.html).  
**Report:** A local reporting intern said one school week saw approximately **18 percent student absence** and around **300 immunizations**, while debunking common misconceptions about contracting H1N1 from pork.  
**Assessment:** **School-specific contemporaneous figures**, not a districtwide infection prevalence study. Absence does not prove laboratory-confirmed infection, and vaccine doses administered in this account do not establish population coverage.

**HN-916 — December 3, 2009 — Black men's unemployment diverges sharply from national recession headline**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 29 — [original economic news brief](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20091203-01.1.29); [Bureau of Labor Statistics](https://www.bls.gov/cps/).  
**Report:** A news brief reports **more than 17 percent seasonally adjusted unemployment among Black men** in an October survey, versus lower rates for white men and women, drawing attention to unequal labor-market effects.  
**Assessment:** **A report of subgroup survey estimates, not 'one in five' for every Black adult or a direct census.** Definition, reference month, seasonal adjustment, and age category must be held constant before comparing BLS figures.

### 2010

**HN-917 — February 5, 2010 — Haiti-earthquake story prints the wrong calendar year**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 2 — [original student report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20100205.1.2); [USGS earthquake origin record](https://earthquake.usgs.gov/earthquakes/eventpage/usp000h60h/origin/detail?code=iscgem14226221&source=us).  
**Report:** The student paper reports Haiti's devastating **magnitude-7.0** earthquake, relief needs, aftershocks and an interim estimate of **200,000 deaths**. In its main paragraph it accidentally dates the event **January 12, 2009**, even though the publication was in February 2010.  
**Assessment:** **A verifiable newspaper date error.** USGS records the earthquake on **January 12, 2010**. The paper's 200,000 figure was an interim, attributed estimate rather than an individually verified death register.

**HN-918 — March 18, 2010 — Calls to cancel Haiti's foreign debt follow the quake**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 14 — [original report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20100318-01.1.14); [USGS quake record](https://earthquake.usgs.gov/earthquakes/eventpage/usp000h60h).  
**Report:** A dispatch describes Representative Maxine Waters pressing the World Bank and other lenders for debt relief to help Haiti address urgent reconstruction costs.  
**Assessment:** **A documented appeal and legislative position, not evidence that every debt was forgiven immediately.** Outcomes would require examining the individual institutions' debt-relief decisions and disbursements.

**HN-919 — April 1, 2010 — Black-press coverage asks whom health reform will reach**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 29 — [original news brief](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20100401-01.1.29); [2010 enactment statute in Congress](https://www.congress.gov/bill/111th-congress/house-bill/3590).  
**Report:** The paper reported passage and signing of President Obama's health-care legislation, then examined its promises for Black patients and uninsured Americans. It referred to the **219–212** House vote.  
**Assessment:** **Passage and advocacy are distinct from measured coverage effects.** The Patient Protection and Affordable Care Act was signed **March 23, 2010**; later enrollment, Medicaid expansion, and implementation need separate data.

**HN-920 — May 20, 2010 — Gulf oil-spill columnist uses a then-current flow estimate**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 11 — [original Marc Morial column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20100520-01.1.11); [NOAA's spill assessment](https://response.restoration.noaa.gov/oil-and-chemical-spills/significant-incidents/deepwater-horizon-oil-spill).  
**Report:** Morial warned of prolonged Gulf Coast environmental and economic damage while saying the well had yet to be capped and repeating an early estimate of **over 200,000 gallons per day**.  
**Assessment:** **Contemporaneous concern and provisional flow-rate reporting, not a final release calculation.** NOAA subsequently assessed approximately **134 million gallons over 87 days** after the April 20 disaster that killed **eleven workers**.

**HN-921 — September 2, 2010 — Nigerian oil-pollution inquiry draws dispute over evidence and funding**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 19 — [original Global Information Network report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20100902-01.1.19); [UNEP's later independent Ogoniland assessment](https://wedocs.unep.org/items/6a723752-f705-41ea-b744-ae2f49f92809).  
**Report:** Environmental groups criticized leaked characterizations of a UN environment study in the Niger Delta, questioned its dependence on oil-industry and government data, and raised concerns over Shell-related funding.  
**Assessment:** **Claims about a leaked draft and competing parties, not a verified finding that the final UN report blamed poor residents for the pollution.** UNEP published its substantive Ogoniland assessment in **2011**, documenting serious contamination and restoration needs; its actual text is the proper comparison.

### 2011

**HN-922 — February 28, 2011 — Tucson shooting prompts a high-school discussion of political rhetoric**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 2 — [original student opinion page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20110228.1.2).  
**Report:** The school paper considers the January shooting of Representative Gabrielle Giffords and others in Tucson, and asks how political language and public safety should be discussed after mass violence.  
**Assessment:** **An editorial response, not a criminal investigation into the shooter's motive.** The causal influence of individual political statements cannot be established from commentary alone.

**HN-923 — May 5, 2011 — Bin Laden's killing dominates the Black press front page**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 1 — [original front-page report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20110505-01.1.1); [presidential statement archived by the White House](https://obamawhitehouse.archives.gov/blog/2011/05/02/osama-bin-laden-dead).  
**Report:** The newspaper covered President Obama's announcement that U.S. forces killed al-Qaeda leader **Osama bin Laden in Abbottabad, Pakistan**, almost a decade after the September 11 attacks, and reported public reactions.  
**Assessment:** **The death and operation were officially announced; some immediate operational details remained subject to revision and later disclosure.** The front page is contemporaneous public reaction, not a complete special-operations record.

**HN-924 — May 5, 2011 — Newspaper prints the president's description of the operation**  
**Source:** *Muncie Times*, p. 3 — [original presidential-address continuation](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=BALLMT20110505-01.1.3); [Obama Presidential Library records](https://www.obamalibrary.gov/research/presidential-records).  
**Report:** The paper reproduced the president's speech framing bin Laden's death in relation to the victims of September 11 and the longer campaign against al-Qaeda.  
**Assessment:** **An official account reproduced by a newspaper, not an independent verification of each operational step.** Presidential claims, intelligence timelines, and subsequent military reporting have different evidentiary roles.

**HN-925 — November 3, 2011 — Occupy protest movement interpreted from a high-school editorial page**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 2 — [original student opinion](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20111103.1.2).  
**Report:** A student editorial portrays Occupy protests as a response to the prolonged recession, inequality, rising education costs, and the influence of wealth in politics, while considering the drawbacks of intentionally leaderless organizing.  
**Assessment:** **A published interpretation of protesters' concerns, not a formal national platform or demographic study.** The editorial compares Occupy to the Tea Party as a political analogy, not as proof that the two movements had equivalent policies.

### 2012

**HN-926 — February 7, 2012 — Brownsville school shooting turns on whether a weapon was real**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 5 — [original student report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20120207.1.5).  
**Report:** A reporter describes police shooting **eighth-grader Jaime González** at Cummings Middle School in Brownsville, Texas, on **January 4**, after responding to a report that he had a gun; the weapon was subsequently described as a pellet gun.  
**Assessment:** **The student article is a secondary account of a fatal police encounter.** It does not establish exactly what responding officers could see or whether their actions were lawful; dispatch audio, investigative findings, and court records remain unreviewed here.

**HN-927 — November 2, 2012 — Pre-election analysis treats swing states as unresolved**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 1 — [original student front-page article](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20121102.1.1).  
**Report:** Editor-in-chief Kate Halliwell assessed the Obama–Romney contest just before voting, treating undecided voters and competitive states as capable of altering the result.  
**Assessment:** **A forecast written before the count, not certified returns.** Its uncertainty belongs to **November 2**, even though the result became clear days later.

**HN-928 — November 21, 2012 — Student press debates the Electoral College after Obama's reelection**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 2 — [original opinion feature](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20121121.1.2).  
**Report:** Two school columnists debate whether the Electoral College adequately represents voters after the presidential election, contrasting institutional stability against critiques of unequal representation.  
**Assessment:** **A paired editorial debate, not a legal change or proven finding of election fraud.** The disagreement shows how younger readers were interpreting election rules immediately afterward.

**HN-929 — November 21, 2012 — Hurricane Sandy's perceived electoral impact is debated**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 4 — [original student post-election discussion](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20121121.1.4); [NOAA Sandy assessment](https://repository.library.noaa.gov/view/noaa/6633).  
**Report:** A post-election school article notes Obama's victory and reports speculation that **Hurricane Sandy** influenced public perceptions during the campaign's final days.  
**Assessment:** **A proposed electoral explanation, not a causal finding.** NOAA documents Sandy's destructive physical impacts and **147 direct fatalities** in the storm's broader affected region; storm consequences and voter behavior must not be conflated.

**HN-930 — December 19, 2012 — Paulsboro bridge derailment releases hazardous vinyl chloride**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 4 — [original student report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20121219.1.4); [NTSB final investigation](https://www.ntsb.gov/investigations/Pages/dca13mr002.aspx).  
**Report:** A story describes a **November 30** freight-train derailment on a New Jersey movable bridge, cars entering Mantua Creek, and the danger posed by vinyl chloride in the damaged cars.  
**Assessment:** **The event and hazardous release are corroborated.** The NTSB ultimately traced the derailment to proceeding past a red signal with bridge-locks not fully engaged and inadequate crew training; the student account's broad environmental fears are not a substitute for exposure measurements.

### 2013

**HN-931 — February 7, 2013 — Student report accurately counts Sandy Hook's 26 school victims**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 4 — [original campus news report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20130207.1.4); [DOJ account of victims](https://www.justice.gov/usao-sdfl/pr/tampa-area-resident-indicted-threatening-sandy-hook-parent).  
**Report:** A high-school writer describes the **December 14, 2012** shooting at Sandy Hook Elementary in Newtown, Connecticut, identifying **twenty children and six adult staff** as the school victims and noting the killing of the attacker's mother separately.  
**Assessment:** **The school victim count is corroborated** but exact terminology about the attack and the perpetrator still requires precision. The 26 were killed at the school; the perpetrator's mother was a separate victim elsewhere.

**HN-932 — February 7, 2013 — School-security follow-up misdates Sandy Hook by three days**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original security discussion](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20130207.1.3); [DOJ date and casualty count](https://www.justice.gov/usao-sdfl/pr/tampa-area-resident-indicted-threatening-sandy-hook-parent).  
**Report:** An article describes safety notices sent to parents and debate over school security after the Newtown massacre, but places the attack on **December 17**, rather than December 14, 2012.  
**Assessment:** **A demonstrable date error within real aftermath reporting.** The massacre occurred **December 14**. This kind of misdating is evidence of imperfect editing/OCR and reporting, not of a second school attack on December 17.

**HN-933 — May 31, 2013 — Boston bombing chronology reconstructed for students**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 1 — [original student report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20130531.1.1); [DOJ indictment account](https://www.justice.gov/usao-ma/pr/federal-grand-jury-returns-30-count-indictment-related-boston-marathon-explosions-and).  
**Report:** A student writer reconstructs the **April 15 Boston Marathon bombing**, subsequent release of suspect photographs, and the confusing days of carjacking, the death of an MIT officer and the manhunt.  
**Assessment:** **Retrospective journalism within weeks of the event, not a complete trial record.** DOJ's June indictment identifies the allegations against Dzhokhar Tsarnaev; charges and early press identification must be kept separate from later judicial findings.

**HN-934 — November 4, 2013 — Student column describes the costs of the federal shutdown**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 7 — [original opinion column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20131104.1.7); [GAO shutdown review](https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-15-86).  
**Report:** A student columnist criticizes the partisan impasse that idled many federal operations and workers during the October government shutdown and worries about future deadlines.  
**Assessment:** **Editorial attribution of political blame is not an official causal finding.** GAO documented a **sixteen-day partial shutdown** from the lapse in appropriations; effects varied by agency, employee category, and specific appropriations authority.

**HN-935 — November 21, 2013 — Indiana charity appeals for Typhoon Haiyan survivors**  
**Source:** *Nappanee Advance-News*, p. 8 — [original local relief appeal](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=NAN20131121.1.8); [United Nations Haiyan retrospective](https://philippines.un.org/en/252171-hct-joint-press-statement-commemorating-10th-anniversary-super-typhoon-haiyan).  
**Report:** A short item solicited funds and essential supplies for survivors of the **November 8** Philippine super-typhoon, describing displacement, destroyed homes, and urgent demands for water, food and shelter.  
**Assessment:** **An appeal is proof of fundraising outreach, not shipment or final distribution.** The UN later recorded **over 6,300 deaths**; the paper's early needs figures and imperfect digitized text should not be used as the final humanitarian ledger.

### 2014

**HN-936 — March 21, 2014 — Student newspaper tracks Ukraine's upheaval and Crimea dispute**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original explanatory article](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20140321.1.3); [UN General Assembly territorial-integrity resolution](https://digitallibrary.un.org/record/767565).  
**Report:** A student feature links protests against President Viktor Yanukovych, his decision against an EU association agreement, and Russia's move into Crimea; its presentation reflects fast-changing reports and contested political narratives.  
**Assessment:** **An early explanation, not a neutral referendum audit.** UN General Assembly Resolution **68/262** subsequently affirmed Ukraine's territorial integrity and called on states not to recognize a change to Crimea's status based on the March vote.

**HN-937 — March 21, 2014 — Commentary treats Putin's move into Crimea as a turning point**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 7 — [original student commentary](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20140321.1.7); [U.S. State Department on Crimea's status](https://2021-2025.state.gov/reports/2018-country-reports-on-human-rights-practices/ukraine/ukraine-crimea/).  
**Report:** A columnist discusses Russian forces entering Crimea and the protests in Kyiv, focusing on the risk of escalation and the extent of President Vladimir Putin's ambitions.  
**Assessment:** **Contemporary interpretation, not privileged evidence of Putin's internal decision-making.** The later purported annexation is widely rejected under Ukraine's territorial integrity; the article's speculative predictions must remain forecasts.

**HN-938 — September 25, 2014 — Ferguson reporting explicitly repeats a rumored quotation**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 7 — [original student column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20140925.1.7); [DOJ federal shooting investigation](https://www.justice.gov/sites/default/files/opa/press-releases/attachments/2015/03/04/doj_report_on_shooting_of_michael_brown_1.pdf).  
**Report:** An opinion article about protests after **Michael Brown's August 9 death** opens with the widely circulated 'Hands up, don't shoot' account but labels it **rumored** and notes conflicting eyewitness statements.  
**Assessment:** **Do not convert the disputed slogan into a verified last quotation.** The later DOJ investigation concluded the evidence did not support federal charges against officer Darren Wilson and found key surrender accounts inconsistent with credible evidence. That finding is distinct from DOJ's separate finding of systemic unconstitutional policing practices in Ferguson.

**HN-939 — October 23, 2014 — Ebola article captures genuine health anxiety and loose epidemic language**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 5 — [original student article](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20141023.1.5); [CDC outbreak history](https://www.cdc.gov/ebola/outbreaks/index.html).  
**Report:** A student report describes widespread fear of the West African Ebola outbreak and reports thousands of infections, using alarming terminology and loosely combining affected African countries.  
**Assessment:** **Fear and early case counts are historical evidence of perception, not evidence that Ebola spreads like airborne seasonal flu or was epidemic across the United States.** CDC records a large West African epidemic but **eleven patients treated in the U.S. and two deaths** during that outbreak.

**HN-940 — October 23, 2014 — Editorial follows Thomas Eric Duncan's U.S. Ebola case**
