**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original student editorial](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20141023.1.3); [CDC outbreak record](https://www.cdc.gov/ebola/outbreaks/index.html).  
**Report:** The paper discusses **Thomas Eric Duncan**, who arrived from Liberia, was initially discharged from a Texas hospital, received an Ebola diagnosis, and died on **October 8**, while considering medical response and public fear.  
**Assessment:** **The central clinical chronology is supported by CDC**, but allegations of widespread domestic transmission are unwarranted. CDC identifies two nurses infected while caring for a patient in the U.S.; separate imported cases do not imply uncontrolled national community spread.

**HN-941 — October 23, 2014 — Student opinion tracks the rise and threats of ISIS**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 7 — [original student column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20141023.1.7); [UN Security Council response](https://press.un.org/en/2015/sc12132.doc.htm).  
**Report:** The column describes Islamic State's territorial expansion in Iraq and Syria, threats against civilians, and violent propaganda, with several large territorial and population figures attributed to other media.  
**Assessment:** **A topical opinion account, not a verified population census or independent order-of-battle assessment.** Geographic control and the number of people under ISIS rule changed quickly; quantitative claims must be dated and separately sourced.

**HN-942 — December 19, 2014 — Ferguson grand-jury decision renews protest and disputed legal descriptions**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 7 — [original student feature](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20141219.1.7); [DOJ's two distinct investigations](https://www.justice.gov/archives/opa/pr/justice-department-announces-findings-two-civil-rights-investigations-ferguson-missouri).  
**Report:** The article describes renewed protests after a grand jury declined to indict officer Darren Wilson, mixing accounts of peaceful demonstrations and property damage. It uses 'trial' loosely in discussing the grand jury.  
**Assessment:** **A grand-jury nonindictment is not an acquittal after a jury trial.** DOJ's March 2015 findings separately declined federal criminal charges in Brown's shooting while documenting a pattern of unconstitutional policing and municipal-court practices. Both outcomes must be retained.

### 2015

**HN-943 — February 2, 2015 — Student reporter details the Charlie Hebdo massacre and free-expression debate**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 1 — [original student front page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20150202.1.1); [UN-linked human-rights year review](https://www.un.org/unispal/wp-content/uploads/2016/08/497ebb62c267a37f85258010004ee9f3_POL1025522016ENGLISH.PDF).  
**Report:** A feature reports the **January 7** attack on the French satirical weekly *Charlie Hebdo*, names the Kouachi brothers, and considers how violent extremism and offense taken at cartoons intersected with freedom of expression.  
**Assessment:** **Real attack, but explanatory discussions of the attackers' beliefs are not proof that an entire religion endorsed violence.** The newsroom assault killed **twelve**; related January Paris attacks bring the wider sequence's total to **seventeen**, a different casualty definition.

**HN-944 — May 1, 2015 — Indiana students take opposing positions on religious-freedom law**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 2 — [original paired student opinions](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20150501.1.2); [Indiana Legislature's 2015 RFRA documents](https://www.indianahouserepublicans.com/sea-101-religious-freedom-restoration-act-rfra-resource-page/).  
**Report:** The school paper debates Indiana's recently enacted Religious Freedom Restoration Act amid concern over LGBT discrimination, private-business practices, boycotts and conscience protections.  
**Assessment:** **Competing legal and ethical arguments, not court holdings.** Indiana enacted **SEA 101 in March**, then adopted **SEA 50's clarifications in April**; characterizations of what businesses could lawfully do must account for the amendment and other applicable laws.

**HN-945 — October 28, 2015 — School-gun debate cites Oregon and Sandy Hook after new shooting**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original student opinion page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20151028.1.3).  
**Report:** A student discussion of gun policy invokes the **October 1 Umpqua Community College** attack and the earlier Sandy Hook massacre to argue over gun availability, school safety and the Second Amendment.  
**Assessment:** **A policy argument built around genuine events, not a controlled study proving the effect of any proposed gun law.** The causal and constitutional claims are the students' positions; outcomes depend on legislation and evidence not examined in this record.

**HN-946 — November 24, 2015 — Syrian refugee crisis enters a student foreign-policy debate**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original student analysis](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20151124.1.3); [UNHCR's July 2015 refugee count](https://www.unhcr.org/news/news-releases/unhcr-total-number-syrian-refugees-exceeds-four-million-first-time).  
**Report:** A student article tracks the Syrian civil war, neighboring countries taking refugees, and the difficult choices facing the United States and Europe while ISIS terrorism dominated debate.  
**Assessment:** **Dated policy interpretation, not a vetting study or evidence that refugees as a group were terrorists.** UNHCR reported **more than four million registered Syrian refugees in neighboring states** by July 2015; refugee registration, asylum applications and internal displacement are distinct categories.

**HN-947 — December 17, 2015 — Opinion-style advertisement connects Paris attacks with a global religious narrative**  
**Source:** *Nappanee Advance-News*, p. 3 — [original page containing 'You deserve a factual look' advocacy text](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=NAN20151217.1.3); [UN condemnation of the November attacks](https://press.un.org/en/2015/sc12132.doc.htm).  
**Report:** An advocacy text on a local news page links the Paris attacks, Jerusalem violence, ISIS and al-Qaeda into a sweeping argument about jihadist ideology. Its explicit 'factual look' heading should not disguise its argumentative form.  
**Assessment:** **Advocacy content, not an independently corroborated newspaper investigation or proof that all episodes have identical actors or motives.** The UN condemned ISIL attacks, including Paris on November 13, but that does not independently verify the advertisement's every generalization.

**HN-948 — December 18, 2015 — Student feature reports the Paris attacks' 130 deaths**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 9 — [original student feature](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20151218.1.9); [UN Security Council statement](https://press.un.org/en/2015/sc12132.doc.htm).  
**Report:** A feature described the coordinated **November 13** attacks in Paris, reporting **130 killed**, police raids and renewed security measures while noting global reactions.  
**Assessment:** **The 130-death figure matches the UN's contemporaneous account.** Early attribution and the number or identity of individual conspirators were active investigative questions; the student feature is not a final prosecution record.

**HN-949 — December 18, 2015 — Russia–Turkey–ISIS oil allegations remain conditional in newspaper debate**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original international-affairs commentary](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20151218.1.3).  
**Report:** A student discussion of the November Turkey–Russia aircraft crisis considers Moscow's accusation that Turkish interests supported ISIS oil trade and explicitly phrases consequences as dependent on **whether Russia could prove** the claim.  
**Assessment:** **A diplomatic accusation and speculative scenario, not an independently established NATO member-state conspiracy.** Proof of specific transactions or officials' culpability would require trade, intelligence and judicial records, not a newspaper's conditional argument.

### 2016

**HN-950 — February 18, 2016 — Indiana Zika report concerns a travel-associated infection, not a local outbreak**  
**Source:** *Nappanee Advance-News*, p. A2 — [original page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=NAN20160218.1.2); [CDC review of U.S. Zika cases](https://www.cdc.gov/zika/zika-cases-us/index.html).  
**Report:** The local paper reported Indiana's recently confirmed Zika case and traced the patient's travel to Haiti. It explained mosquito transmission and the new uncertainty surrounding the virus.  
**Assessment:** **The reported case should not be equated with sustained mosquito-borne transmission in Indiana.** The article is a useful record of rapidly evolving concern; its implied predictions about future spread were not findings.

**HN-951 — March 3, 2016 — Flint's contaminated tap water reaches a student newspaper**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 4 — [original student article](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20160303.1.4); [EPA inspector-general review](https://www.epa.gov/sites/default/files/2018-07/documents/_epaoig_20180719-18-p-0221.pdf).  
**Report:** A Lafayette student reporter described Flint residents' alarm after discovering lead in drinking water, including the effects on children and trust in public agencies.  
**Assessment:** **The lead contamination was real; the text is a student synthesis, not an exposure study.** A later EPA inspector-general investigation documented delayed federal response. Broad health consequences cannot be assigned to each household from this article alone.

**HN-952 — June 12, 2016 — Early Pulse nightclub casualty reporting contains a counting trap**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 12 — [contemporaneous eyewitness reconstruction](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2016/jun/12/orlando-nightclub-shooting-how-attack-unfolded).  
**Report:** Survivors described fleeing gunfire at Orlando's Pulse nightclub while families waited for word about people inside. Early updates referred to around 50 fatalities.  
**Assessment:** **The final victim count is 49, with the gunman separately killed.** Some first-day totals included the attacker, and initial witness timelines were incomplete. Do not read preliminary totals as conflicting victim lists.

**HN-953 — June 24, 2016 — Brexit referendum result was not immediate EU withdrawal**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 24 — [original result-night report](https://www.theguardian.com/politics/2016/jun/24/britain-votes-for-brexit-eu-referendum-david-cameron).  
**Report:** The paper described the surprise majority for leaving the European Union, turbulence in sterling and the political consequences for Prime Minister David Cameron.  
**Assessment:** **A referendum vote, not completed legal withdrawal.** The United Kingdom formally left the EU on January 31, 2020; later negotiations and trade arrangements should not be projected into what the June 2016 article could know.

**HN-954 — November 9, 2016 — Election-night Trump victory before certifications**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, November 9 — [contemporaneous election report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2016/nov/09/donald-trump-wins-us-election-news).  
**Report:** Donald Trump's unexpected presidential victory over Hillary Clinton prompted analysis of the electoral map, polling failures and reactions from both campaigns.  
**Assessment:** **Election-night projections preceded the state certifications and Electoral College vote.** Claims about voter motives are analysis rather than individual voters' documented testimony; this result does not establish any separate allegation of fraud.

### 2017

**HN-955 — May 12, 2017 — Ransomware makes hospital IT failures an international crisis**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, May 12 — [original worldwide cyberattack reporting](https://www.theguardian.com/technology/2017/may/12/global-cyber-attack-ransomware-nsa-uk-nhs).  
**Report:** The spreading WannaCry ransomware attack locked computers and disrupted National Health Service facilities in Britain while similar incidents emerged in other countries.  
**Assessment:** **Confirmed disruption, with scope still changing hour by hour.** Initial estimates of affected countries or facilities were not a completed global incident count; disruption did not mean that every patient file had been stolen.

**HN-956 — August 12, 2017 — Charlottesville: a murder charge is not yet a verdict**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, first published August 12 — [report on vehicle attack and charges](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2017/aug/12/virginia-unite-the-right-rally-protest-violence).  
**Report:** After a vehicle struck counterprotesters at the white-supremacist Unite the Right rally, the paper reported one death, injuries, James Fields's arrest and separate deaths of two police officers in a helicopter crash.  
**Assessment:** **Separate the car attack from the helicopter crash, and the contemporaneous charge from later convictions.** The victim of the car attack was Heather Heyer; early accounts sometimes withheld her identity pending notification.

**HN-957 — August 28, 2017 — Harvey's rapidly changing flood and shelter estimates**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, August 28 — [contemporaneous Texas/Louisiana report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2017/aug/28/harvey-texas-louisiana-rain-rescue-houston).  
**Report:** Rescuers struggled through severe flooding in and around Houston as authorities warned that roughly 30,000 people might require shelter.  
**Assessment:** **A forecast of potential shelter need, not a verified count of evacuees already in shelters.** Harvey's rainfall, flooding, deaths and costs required later geographic and agency reconciliation.

**HN-958 — September 20, 2017 — Maria leaves Puerto Rico facing island-wide electricity failure**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, September 20 — [original hurricane and blackout report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2017/sep/20/life-threatening-winds-and-floods-hit-puerto-rico-as-maria-makes-landfall).  
**Report:** Reports from Puerto Rico described life-threatening wind, flooding, rescue operations and a nearly total collapse in electrical service as Hurricane Maria crossed the island.  
**Assessment:** **The immediate blackout was extensively reported; the full indirect death toll was not knowable that day.** Do not substitute first-day disaster figures for later excess-mortality estimates.

**HN-959 — October 20, 2017 — A local Las Vegas shooting survivor story misdates the attack**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 7 — [original student feature](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20171020.1.7).  
**Report:** A Lafayette paper's locally connected account conveyed the human shock of the Mandalay Bay concert shooting and described firing from the hotel onto concertgoers. Its opening says **October 2** and prints “32th floor.”  
**Assessment:** **The attack took place October 1, 2017, from the 32nd floor.** This is a revealing period-newspaper error, not evidence of two incidents. Early counts and local recollections must be distinguished from the finalized investigation.

### 2018

**HN-960 — February 14, 2018 — Parkland's first-day reports: 17 people dead**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, first published February 14 — [original school-shooting report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2018/feb/14/florida-shooting-school-latest-news-stoneman-douglas).  
**Report:** Witnesses and authorities described an attack at Marjory Stoneman Douglas High School in Parkland, Florida, killing 17 people, as survivors and parents tried to locate students.  
**Assessment:** **Confirmed event and death toll; early accounts of police response were incomplete.** Later criminal proceedings and response inquiries should be consulted separately from the first day's emergency narrative.

**HN-961 — March 22, 2018 — Indiana students report their own post-Parkland walkout movement**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 1 — [original student newspaper front page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20180322.1.1).  
**Report:** The student feature headed “Enough is enough” connected Parkland to school walkouts and teenagers organizing public protest; it repeated an early media claim of **17 school shootings** in the year to date.  
**Assessment:** **The walkout movement is a genuine contemporaneous development.** The reported number of “school shootings” depends on contested event definitions and is not adopted here as a settled national statistic.

**HN-962 — June 16, 2018 — Family-separation total is a six-week snapshot**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 16 — [period report on separated children](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2018/jun/16/children-separated-parents-border-trump-administration).  
**Report:** Government figures showed nearly 2,000 children separated from parents at the U.S.–Mexico border during a six-week period as the administration's immigration enforcement policy came under scrutiny.  
**Assessment:** **A specified period and policy count, not the all-time number or a count of children permanently separated.** Later reunification audits used different populations and dates.

**HN-963 — July 8, 2018 — Four of 12 Thai cave-trapped boys initially rescued**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, July 8 — [original staged-rescue report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2018/jul/08/thailand-cave-rescue-begins-four-boys-freed).  
**Report:** Divers escorted the first four boys out of the flooded Tham Luang cave while the rest of the football team and coach remained inside awaiting a further operation.  
**Assessment:** **The four-person tally was an interim rescue-stage number.** All 12 boys and the coach ultimately emerged; treating this article as the final outcome would falsely leave survivors missing.

**HN-964 — October 29, 2018 — New Boeing 737 Max crashes into Java Sea**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, first published October 28 (U.S. time), reporting October 29 local crash — [original Lion Air Flight 610 account](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2018/oct/29/lion-air-plane-lost-contact-indonesia-flight-jt610-jakarta).  
**Report:** Lion Air Flight JT610 disappeared shortly after departure from Jakarta; the first reporting emphasized that the Boeing 737 Max 8 airframe was relatively new.  
**Assessment:** **The cause was not settled when the article was written.** The subsequent investigation identified a chain including erroneous angle-of-attack data, MCAS behavior, training and maintenance factors. An early “lost contact” bulletin cannot bear a definitive mechanical-cause claim.

**HN-965 — November 9, 2018 — Paradise's Camp Fire: nine initially reported dead**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, November 9 — [firsthand account from Paradise, California](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2018/nov/09/california-wildfire-paradise-chico-camp-fire); [CAL FIRE's final incident record](https://www.fire.ca.gov/incidents/2018/11/8/camp-fire).  
**Report:** Evacuees described a town burned almost beyond recognition; local authorities had confirmed at least nine deaths and thousands of destroyed structures while crews searched.  
**Assessment:** **A preliminary count during an active fire.** California's eventual official Camp Fire death toll was 85; the scale of the disaster was not captured by initial reports.

### 2019

**HN-966 — March 10, 2019 — Second fatal 737 Max crash triggers scrutiny, not a same-day cause finding**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, March 10 — [original Ethiopian Airlines Flight 302 report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2019/mar/10/ethiopian-airlines-says-kenya-flight-with-157-onboard-has-crashed).  
**Report:** Ethiopian Airlines Flight 302 crashed after leaving Addis Ababa with 157 people aboard, prompting questions about a model already involved in the Lion Air disaster.  
**Assessment:** **All 157 died; the crash was confirmed.** The technical relationship between the two disasters was the subject of subsequent flight-data analysis and investigations, not established by the first article's speculation.

**HN-967 — April 15, 2019 — Notre-Dame's spire falls, but the cathedral is not lost in entirety**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, April 15 — [original Paris fire coverage](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2019/apr/15/notre-dame-fire-paris-france-cathedral).  
**Report:** Fire devastated the roof of Notre-Dame Cathedral, destroyed its spire and threatened the bell towers; firefighters later said the two towers had been saved.  
**Assessment:** **Roof and spire destruction did not mean the entire stone structure collapsed.** The fire's precise origin was still under investigation, and images of the burning roof were not proof of an intentional attack.

**HN-968 — June 12, 2019 — Hong Kong extradition protests meet tear gas**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, first published June 11 and updated June 12 — [original protest reporting](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2019/jun/11/hong-kong-extradition-law-protesters-rally-outside-government-offices).  
**Report:** Demonstrators protested a proposed bill permitting extraditions to mainland China; police used crowd-control weapons during escalating confrontations around the legislature.  
**Assessment:** **A bill under contest, not a law already implemented.** Protesters' descriptions, police justifications and later legal outcomes are different evidentiary categories.

**HN-969 — August 3, 2019 — El Paso: early Walmart shooting toll was provisional**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, August 3 — [original El Paso shooting report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2019/aug/03/el-paso-shooting-texas-walmart).  
**Report:** Survivors described gunfire at a Walmart in El Paso and authorities announced an early fatality count while beginning a possible hate-crime and terrorism investigation.  
**Assessment:** **Initial casualty totals subsequently rose and legal charges were later adjudicated.** A reported manifesto or suspected motive was initially investigative evidence, not a verdict or proof that every circulating text was authentic.

**HN-970 — September 26, 2019 — Whistleblower allegations become a public document**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, September 26 — [original complaint coverage](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2019/sep/26/slug-trump-ukraine-latest-whistleblower-complaint-2020-election).  
**Report:** Publication of a whistleblower complaint alleged that the White House restricted access to records of President Trump's July conversation with Ukraine's president, prompting an impeachment inquiry.  
**Assessment:** **A published allegation and an official inquiry are not equivalent to a criminal finding.** Distinguish the complaint, the released call memorandum, later sworn testimony and the constitutional impeachment process.

### 2020

**HN-971 — March 11, 2020 — WHO calls COVID-19 a pandemic**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, March 11 — [original WHO announcement coverage](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2020/mar/11/who-declares-coronavirus-pandemic).  
**Report:** WHO Director-General Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus characterized the global COVID-19 outbreak as a pandemic and criticized insufficient preparedness and action.  
**Assessment:** **The announcement was a description of global spread, not the day the disease began.** Early case counts were provisional and affected by uneven testing, case definitions and reporting delays.

**HN-972 — May 28, 2020 — George Floyd protests expose competing first accounts**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, May 28 — [original Minneapolis protest and federal inquiry report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2020/may/28/minneapolis-george-floyd-death-protests).  
**Report:** Protesters demanded accountability after video captured Minneapolis officer Derek Chauvin kneeling on George Floyd's neck; the Justice Department said the case was a priority and crowds gathered across the city.  
**Assessment:** **The video, charging records, trial evidence and later conviction supersede first police statements.** Protest-related fires and other actions should not be imputed to every demonstrator.

**HN-973 — August 4, 2020 — Beirut port explosion: 78 deaths was an initial count**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, August 4 — [first-day blast report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2020/aug/04/huge-explosion-beirut-lebanon-shatters-windows-rocks-buildings).  
**Report:** An enormous blast at Beirut's port shattered buildings and overwhelmed hospitals; early officials cited at least 78 dead and pointed to a large stockpile of ammonium nitrate.  
**Assessment:** **The provisional death count rose above 200.** Ammonium nitrate storage and official warnings became central investigative questions; the initial report did not establish which individuals were criminally responsible.

**HN-974 — October 15, 2020 — Local Black Lives Matter debate printed as opposing opinions**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 2 — [original student opinion page](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20201015.1.2).  
**Report:** Student contributors debated whether 2020 Black Lives Matter protests advanced justice or intensified political division, in a deliberately contrasting opinion-page format.  
**Assessment:** **Two opinions are not two independent factual investigations.** The page records local reaction to national protests, not proof of either sweeping proposition about the movement or everyone participating in it.

**HN-975 — November 2, 2020 — Student COVID article invokes the Great Barrington Declaration**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 5 — [original student article](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20201102.1.5).  
**Report:** A student review of Donald Trump's pandemic response cited the October Great Barrington Declaration as a proposed alternative to broad restrictions and described public disagreement over protections.  
**Assessment:** **The declaration was a policy advocacy statement, not a completed Oxford clinical experiment or consensus position.** Its assertions about population immunity and shielding must not be represented as established outcomes.

**HN-976 — November 7, 2020 — Biden declared winner while vote tabulation continues**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, November 7 — [period election call](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2020/nov/07/joe-biden-wins-us-election-donald-trump-loses-final-result-2020).  
**Report:** Major news organizations called the presidency for Joe Biden after key-state counts made the outcome clear; President Trump disputed the result.  
**Assessment:** **A news-organization projection precedes formal certification.** Subsequent recounts, litigation and the Electoral College are distinct steps; Trump's objections were allegations, not evidence that the certified result was fraudulent.

### 2021

**HN-977 — January 6, 2021 — Assault on the Capitol interrupts electoral-vote count**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, January 6 — [first-day account from Congress](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2021/jan/06/us-capitol-lockdown-senate-trump-supporters-protesters-police).  
**Report:** A pro-Trump mob forced its way into the U.S. Capitol during the congressional counting of presidential electoral votes; lawmakers and staff sheltered as police tried to secure the building.  
**Assessment:** **The breach and interruption are documented; first-day descriptions of participants, motives, fatalities and responsibility remained incomplete.** Subsequent prosecutions and congressional inquiries must be distinguished from early crowd reports.

**HN-978 — March 24, 2021 — A grounded megaship stops Suez Canal traffic**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, March 24 — [original canal-shipping report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2021/mar/24/suez-canal-traffic-jam-builds-as-work-to-move-megaship-continues).  
**Report:** The container ship *Ever Given* lay crosswise in the Suez Canal while dredgers and tugs struggled to free it and queues of commercial ships grew.  
**Assessment:** **A contemporaneous snapshot of ships held up, not a finished economic-damage calculation.** The vessel was later freed; early estimates of trade losses were projections and should not be totaled as audited losses.

**HN-979 — June 24, 2021 — Surfside collapse: 99 unaccounted for is not 99 confirmed deaths**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 24 — [original Miami-area condominium collapse report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2021/jun/24/emergency-operation-under-way-after-miami-building-collapse); [NIST's later investigation findings](https://www.nist.gov/disaster-and-failure-studies/video-transcript-nists-technical-findings-ncst-investigation-june-24).  
**Report:** Rescuers searched the Champlain Towers South wreckage in Surfside, Florida, as officials reported one confirmed death and about 99 people then unaccounted for.  
**Assessment:** **'Unaccounted for' included people whose status was unresolved, not a confirmed victim register.** The eventual death toll was 98; engineering causation remained the subject of a longer investigation.

**HN-980 — August 15, 2021 — Kabul falls far sooner than many forecasts**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, August 15 — [original Kabul-collapse reporting](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2021/aug/15/the-fall-of-kabul-a-20-year-mission-collapses-in-a-single-day).  
**Report:** Taliban fighters entered Kabul as the Afghan government collapsed, President Ashraf Ghani departed and civilians scrambled to find evacuation routes.  
**Assessment:** **The sudden collapse is documented; precise motives, evacuation numbers and long-term outcomes were not established that day.** A fall of government does not substantiate every public claim about individual Afghan citizens' allegiances.

**HN-981 — September 27, 2021 — Student foreign-affairs columnist disputes Taliban promises**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 5 — [original student column](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20210927.1.5).  
**Report:** A student column described August's fall of Kabul, distressing evacuation images and skepticism toward Taliban assurances of a more moderate government.  
**Assessment:** **The source is overtly opinionated commentary drawing on contemporary reports.** Its strongest judgments are the writer's interpretations; videos of desperate evacuees do not establish the fate of every person visible in them.

**HN-982 — November 26, 2021 — Omicron's unknowns at the time of designation**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, November 26 — [period explainer about the newly named variant](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2021/nov/26/vaccine-resistant-what-scientists-know-new-covid-variant).  
**Report:** Researchers and public-health officials assessed a newly identified SARS-CoV-2 variant with numerous mutations and raised questions about transmissibility and vaccine protection.  
**Assessment:** **Potential immune escape was an early scientific concern, not proof that vaccines offered no benefit.** Subsequent population data and laboratory studies were required to estimate severity and protection.

### 2022

**HN-983 — February 1, 2022 — Student paper warned of a Russian invasion before it occurred**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original international-affairs commentary](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20220201.1.3).  
**Report:** Amid Russian troop concentrations along Ukraine's borders, a student writer considered an invasion increasingly likely and summarized NATO, sanctions and American military readiness debates.  
**Assessment:** **A correctly anticipated possibility, not evidence the writer possessed privileged intelligence.** The column's simplified explanation of Russia's objectives remains an interpretation, and at the February 1 publication date a full-scale invasion had not occurred.

**HN-984 — February 24, 2022 — Russia launches full-scale invasion of Ukraine**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, February 24 — [contemporaneous dawn-airstrike coverage](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2022/feb/24/airstrikes-dawn-russia-begins-war-aggression-with-ukraine-vladimir-putin).  
**Report:** Explosions were reported in Ukrainian cities as Russian forces opened a broad assault after President Vladimir Putin announced a military operation.  
**Assessment:** **Distinguish the 2022 full-scale invasion from Russia's 2014 seizure of Crimea and the existing Donbas war.** First-day claims of casualty counts, seized territory and military success were preliminary and often contested.

**HN-985 — April 4, 2022 — Indiana commentary questions the effects of Russian oil sanctions**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 3 — [original student opinion article](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20220404.1.3).  
**Report:** The paper discussed President Biden's March 8 prohibition on Russian oil imports and weighed economic harm to Russia against possible U.S. fuel-price consequences.  
**Assessment:** **Sanctions policy and its predicted effectiveness are different claims.** The commentary provides a contemporaneous view, not a controlled estimate of how much any price change or later Russian decision was caused by this particular ban.

**HN-986 — May 24, 2022 — Uvalde's first reporting precedes review of police delay**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, first published May 24 — [original Robb Elementary shooting report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2022/may/24/texas-school-shooting-uvalde-robb-elementary); [later Justice Department critical-incident review](https://www.justice.gov/archives/opa/pr/justice-department-releases-report-its-critical-incident-review-response-mass-shooting-robb).  
**Report:** Authorities confirmed the deaths of 19 children and two adults at Robb Elementary School in Uvalde, Texas, while parents sought information and law enforcement described the response.  
**Assessment:** **The 21 victims are established; the first police chronology was not reliable as a final account.** The later DOJ review found serious failures in the law-enforcement response, making source date crucial when comparing narratives.

**HN-987 — June 24, 2022 — Supreme Court reverses Roe in Dobbs**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 24 — [original decision coverage](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2022/jun/24/roe-v-wade-overturned-abortion-summary-supreme-court).  
**Report:** The U.S. Supreme Court ruled in *Dobbs v. Jackson Women's Health Organization* that the Constitution did not guarantee a right to abortion, overturning *Roe v. Wade* and *Planned Parenthood v. Casey*.  
**Assessment:** **A real change in federal constitutional doctrine, not an immediate nationwide ban enacted by the Court.** Subsequent restrictions and exceptions varied by state and changed through further litigation.

**HN-988 — September 17, 2022 — Mahsa Amini's death sparks protest amid disputed police account**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, September 17 — [early Iran protest reporting](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2022/sep/17/iran-protests-death-kurdish-woman-mahsaa-amini-morality-police).  
**Report:** Demonstrators protested after the death of Mahsa (Jina) Amini, a Kurdish Iranian woman detained by morality police over alleged dress-code violations. Relatives disputed official explanations of her collapse.  
**Assessment:** **Her custody and death and the resulting protests are established.** The immediate physical mechanism and any individual criminal culpability were disputed; do not silently turn either side's early description into a final forensic verdict.

**HN-989 — November 11, 2022 — Cryptocurrency exchange FTX enters bankruptcy**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, November 11 — [original collapse report](https://www.theguardian.com/technology/2022/nov/11/cryptocurrency-exchange-ftx-files-for-bankruptcy-protection-in-us).  
**Report:** FTX and related entities sought U.S. bankruptcy protection amid a liquidity crisis, with founder Sam Bankman-Fried stepping down as chief executive.  
**Assessment:** **Bankruptcy filing is documented, but reported balance-sheet gaps were early estimates.** Subsequent criminal trials, recoveries and creditor distributions are separate later records, not all known on November 11.

### 2023

**HN-990 — February 6, 2023 — Turkey–Syria earthquake death reports begin amid rescue uncertainty**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, February 6 — [first-day earthquake report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2023/feb/06/turkey-earthquake-syria-what-we-know-so-far); [USGS seismic sequence](https://www.usgs.gov/news/featured-story/m78-and-m75-kahramanmaras-earthquake-sequence-near-nurdagi-turkey-turkiye).  
**Report:** Rescuers searched collapsed buildings after a magnitude-7.8 earthquake and a second major earthquake in southern Turkey and northern Syria. The initial toll was rising rapidly.  
**Assessment:** **USGS confirms the magnitude-7.8 and magnitude-7.5 sequence.** Initial death and building-loss numbers were snapshots; the final humanitarian scale depended on prolonged recovery and revised registers.

**HN-991 — February 6, 2023 — East Palestine officials prepare controlled chemical release**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, February 6 — [original Ohio derailment report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2023/feb/06/ohio-train-derailment-evacuation-warning); [NTSB later investigation findings](https://www.ntsb.gov/news/press-releases/pages/nr20240625.aspx).  
**Report:** After a Norfolk Southern train derailed on February 3 in East Palestine, Ohio, residents were evacuated as officials planned to release and burn vinyl chloride from damaged tank cars to avert a feared explosion.  
**Assessment:** **The derailment and planned vent-and-burn were distinct events.** The NTSB later found an overheated, failed wheel bearing caused the derailment and scrutinized the decision-making around the chemical release.

**HN-992 — March 20, 2023 — Student report captures East Palestine's early technical theory**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 5 — [original Lafayette student report](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20230320.1.5); [NTSB final findings](https://www.ntsb.gov/news/press-releases/pages/nr20240625.aspx).  
**Report:** The paper reported **38 derailed railcars**, **11 carrying hazardous materials**, concern about vinyl chloride and a suspected bearing-overheat failure as investigations continued.  
**Assessment:** **An unusually technical student report that substantially anticipated the NTSB's later bearing-failure finding.** Its chemical-hazard descriptions are not individual residents' exposure measurements, and the timing matters: the inquiry was not final in March 2023.

**HN-993 — June 22, 2023 — Titan search becomes catastrophic-implosion recovery**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 22 — [original Coast Guard announcement coverage](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2023/jun/22/titanic-sub-titan-debris-field-search-area-latest).  
**Report:** After days of searching for OceanGate's *Titan* submersible near the *Titanic* wreck, the U.S. Coast Guard announced a debris field consistent with catastrophic implosion and that the five people aboard were believed dead.  
**Assessment:** **No credible rescue prospect remained after the debris identification.** Earlier media attention to underwater noises and theoretical remaining oxygen reflected information available before wreckage was found, not proof the crew survived for days.

**HN-994 — August 10, 2023 — Lahaina's destruction seen while fatalities still uncertain**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, August 10 — [original Maui fire and survivor reporting](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2023/aug/10/lahaina-maui-wildfire-damage).  
**Report:** Accounts from Maui described Lahaina's devastated historic center, damaged neighborhoods, desperate evacuations and a rapidly rising toll as fires continued to burn.  
**Assessment:** **A confirmed major wildfire catastrophe, with early victim counts subject to later identification and reconciliation.** Accounts of individual escape routes should not be generalized to every neighborhood or turned into an unproven ignition finding.

**HN-995 — September 7, 2023 — Student article links Canadian fires to U.S. lumber prices**  
**Source:** *Jefferson Booster*, p. 5 — [original student economic feature](https://newspapers.library.in.gov/?a=d&d=JFBSTR20230907.1.5).  
**Report:** The paper connected Canada's extensive 2023 wildfires to potential softwood-lumber supply pressure and costs for American construction, citing Reuters and a large import-share estimate.  
**Assessment:** **The article offers an economic hypothesis, not an identified causal price study.** Its “80%” import share, harvest impacts and future price prediction require matching trade datasets, dates and denominators before use as facts.

**HN-996 — October 7, 2023 — First-day account of Hamas attack and Israeli response**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, October 7 — [original report from Israel and Gaza](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2023/oct/07/hamas-launches-surprise-attack-on-israel-as-palestinian-gunmen-reported-in-south).  
**Report:** Hamas-led attackers crossed from Gaza into southern Israel, launched rockets and attacked communities; Israeli forces responded as authorities and families struggled to determine the fate of missing and abducted people.  
**Assessment:** **The attack and unfolding war were real; first-day casualty, hostage and responsibility totals were necessarily provisional.** Separate later investigations are needed for each alleged atrocity, military target, and civilian-casualty claim.

### 2024

**HN-997 — January 6, 2024 — Alaska Airlines door-plug loss reveals a new Boeing crisis**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, January 6 — [original grounding announcement](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2024/jan/06/alaska-airlines-grounds-boeing-737-max-9-planes-after-mid-air-window-blowout); [NTSB accident investigation](https://www.ntsb.gov/investigations/pages/DCA24MA063.aspx).  
**Report:** A panel covering an unused emergency exit separated from Alaska Airlines Flight 1282 shortly after takeoff, leaving a hole in the Boeing 737 Max 9. The plane returned safely and U.S. regulators ordered temporary inspections and groundings.  
**Assessment:** **Confirmed structural separation, not an explosion or a passenger opening the door.** The NTSB later identified manufacturing, training and oversight problems; the January report could not yet know the full missing-bolt sequence.

**HN-998 — March 26, 2024 — Cargo vessel *Dali* brings down Baltimore bridge**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, March 26 — [first-day collapse report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2024/mar/26/baltimore-francis-scott-key-bridge-collapses-after-boat-collision); [NTSB investigation](https://www.ntsb.gov/investigations/Pages/DCA24MM031.aspx).  
**Report:** A container ship struck a support of the Francis Scott Key Bridge in Baltimore in the early hours; the span collapsed and rescuers searched the Patapsco River for workers on the bridge.  
**Assessment:** **Six workers ultimately died.** The first-day description of people missing and early theories of equipment failure were not the finalized marine investigation. The collision was a transport catastrophe, not evidence of an attack merely because footage was dramatic.

**HN-999 — April 8, 2024 — North American eclipse witnessed across three countries**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, April 8 — [original on-the-ground eclipse coverage](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2024/apr/08/total-solar-eclipse-path-map).  
**Report:** Millions watched totality pass over parts of Mexico, the United States and Canada, with reporters describing unusual darkness and weather-dependent viewing conditions along the path.  
**Assessment:** **An astronomical event with calculable path and timing.** Cloudy locations and anecdotal observations should not be mistaken for changes in the predicted eclipse track or signs of an anomalous celestial event.

**HN-1000 — July 13, 2024 — Butler rally shooting: first accounts amid an active investigation**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, July 13 — [original Pennsylvania rally report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/article/2024/jul/13/trump-rally-gun-shots-pennsylvania).  
**Report:** Shots rang out during Donald Trump's campaign rally in Butler, Pennsylvania. Trump was wounded in the ear, one attendee was killed, others were injured and the shooter was killed by security personnel.  
**Assessment:** **A documented assassination attempt.** The early report did not establish the gunman's complete motive or demonstrate any wider conspiracy; subsequent Secret Service and congressional reviews examined security failures.

**HN-1001 — September 29, 2024 — Helene leaves catastrophic inland flooding**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, September 29 — [original flood reporting](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2024/sep/29/hurricane-helene-historic-flooding-global-heating-fema); [National Weather Service storm summary](https://www.weather.gov/media/gsp/TropicalEventSummary/PSHGSP_2024AL09_Helene_Summary.pdf).  
**Report:** As Helene's remnants swept through the southeastern United States, communities in western North Carolina and surrounding states faced extreme rainfall, swollen rivers, washed-out roads and deaths far from the coast.  
**Assessment:** **A hurricane's effects can continue inland after winds diminish.** Early counts of missing people, rescues, infrastructure damage and casualties changed; any claim about the precise climate-attributable fraction requires dedicated attribution research beyond this report.

**HN-1002 — November 6, 2024 — Trump wins return to the White House**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, November 6 — [original election-night report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2024/nov/06/donald-trump-wins-us-presidential-election).  
**Report:** Major media organizations projected that Donald Trump had defeated Kamala Harris in the presidential election, returning him to office after his 2020 defeat.  
**Assessment:** **A projection later confirmed through state certifications and the Electoral College.** Election-night analysis of why voters made their choices is interpretation; reporting the result is not a finding about the validity of unrelated allegations or future policy outcomes.

### 2025

**HN-1003 — January 8, 2025 — Los Angeles neighborhoods flee fast-moving fires**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, January 8 — [original Los Angeles wildfire coverage](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2025/jan/08/los-angeles-wildfires).  
**Report:** Residents of Pacific Palisades and surrounding Los Angeles neighborhoods recounted frightening evacuations as major wind-driven fires consumed homes and public buildings.  
**Assessment:** **The fires and destruction were documented, but early loss estimates, maps and theories of ignition were evolving.** The contemporaneous eyewitness article alone cannot establish a final attribution of responsibility or an audited cost.

**HN-1004 — January 29, 2025 — Passenger jet and Army helicopter collide over the Potomac**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, first published January 29 — [original river-rescue report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2025/jan/29/aircraft-crash-ronald-reagan-airport-virginia); [NTSB collision investigation](https://www.ntsb.gov/investigations/Pages/DCA25MA108.aspx).  
**Report:** A regional American Airlines flight collided with a U.S. Army helicopter on approach to Ronald Reagan Washington National Airport. Rescuers searched the Potomac River overnight for people aboard both aircraft.  
**Assessment:** **All 67 people aboard the two aircraft died, as later established.** The late-night paper described a search still under way; it was not a complete finding about air-traffic-control practices, military route design or crew culpability.

**HN-1005 — February 26, 2025 — Measles death alarms west Texas amid expanding outbreak**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, February 26 — [contemporaneous measles reporting](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2025/feb/26/texas-measles-outbreak-cases).  
**Report:** Health officials reported the death of an unvaccinated child during a growing measles outbreak centered in west Texas, prompting concern about transmission in undervaccinated communities.  
**Assessment:** **An outbreak and fatal case were documented.** A first-day case total does not represent the outbreak's eventual size, and medical risk cannot be inferred for an individual merely from membership in a community.

**HN-1006 — March 2, 2025 — Firefly's Blue Ghost makes an upright lunar landing**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, March 2 — [original mission imagery and landing report](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2025/mar/02/private-firefly-aerospace-spacecraft-blue-ghost-makes-successful-upright-moon-landing).  
**Report:** The privately built Blue Ghost lander touched down on the Moon and returned pictures, advancing commercial delivery of scientific instruments to the lunar surface.  
**Assessment:** **Successful reported touchdown, not proof that all planned instrument operations were completed at the moment of landing.** Distinguish this upright mission from earlier private lunar attempts with different landing outcomes.

**HN-1007 — April 21, 2025 — Pope Francis dies at 88**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, April 21 — [original Vatican succession reporting](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2025/apr/21/pope-francis-dies).  
**Report:** The Vatican announced the death of Pope Francis, the first Jesuit pontiff, triggering mourning and the procedures for choosing his successor.  
**Assessment:** **His death and papal office are established.** Assessments of his legacy and projected direction of the next pontificate were contemporary opinion and speculation, not facts about an as-yet-unelected successor.

**HN-1008 — May 8, 2025 — Robert Prevost elected Pope Leo XIV**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, May 8 — [original conclave announcement](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2025/may/08/white-smoke-signals-election-of-new-roman-catholic-pope).  
**Report:** White smoke signaled a conclave result, and Cardinal Robert Francis Prevost was introduced as Pope Leo XIV, the first pope born in the United States.  
**Assessment:** **A confirmed election and chosen papal name.** Predictions of the new pope's policies based on his nationality or previous roles were not conclusions of the conclave itself.

**HN-1009 — June 12, 2025 — Air India Dreamliner crash: one survivor emerges**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 12 — [original Ahmedabad crash reconstruction](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2025/jun/12/how-air-india-plane-crash-unfolded).  
**Report:** Air India Flight 171 crashed shortly after takeoff from Ahmedabad toward London, hitting buildings near the airport. Amid reports of mass fatalities, authorities identified a survivor from the aircraft.  
**Assessment:** **A confirmed catastrophic aviation accident; the one surviving passenger was not evidence that many more occupants escaped.** Early witness interpretations of the aircraft's behavior were not a final causal finding, and people killed on the ground require separate accounting.

**HN-1010 — July 4, 2025 — Texas flash floods strike a girls' summer camp**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, first published July 4 — [original Guadalupe River flood report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2025/jul/04/texas-flooding-disaster-declarations).  
**Report:** Dangerous overnight flooding struck central Texas, with authorities reporting at least 24 deaths at an early stage and searching for children unaccounted for at Camp Mystic along the Guadalupe River.  
**Assessment:** **Those figures were initial emergency reports, not the final death toll or missing-person roster.** Subsequent rescue and recovery reports must be used to establish individual fates and any failures in local warning systems.

### 2026 (through October 22)

**HN-1011 — January 3, 2026 — US operation seizes Venezuela's president**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, January 3 — [first-day Caracas attack and capture report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/jan/03/explosions-reported-venezuela-caracas).  
**Report:** After explosions and military activity around Caracas, President Donald Trump announced that US forces had captured Venezuelan president Nicolás Maduro and his wife, Cilia Flores, and removed them from the country. The US government announced criminal charges against the couple.  
**Assessment:** **The attack, seizure and US announcement were events; claims about the raid's legality, authority to govern Venezuela and ultimate criminal liability were not resolved by an initial news dispatch.** This record does not imply a lawful transfer of Venezuelan sovereignty.

**HN-1012 — January 8, 2026 — Minneapolis shooting prompts scrutiny of an ICE officer's account**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, January 8 — [contemporary reporting on Renee Nicole Good's death](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/jan/08/ice-agent-minneapolis-shooting).  
**Report:** An ICE officer shot and killed 37-year-old Renee Nicole Good during a federal immigration operation in Minneapolis on January 7; reporting the next day identified the officer through matching court records as Jonathan E Ross. The shooting produced demonstrations and conflicting official and witness accounts.  
**Assessment:** **Her death and the ensuing dispute are documented.** Neither the government's self-defense characterization nor opponents' interpretations should be silently treated as a completed forensic or judicial finding.

**HN-1013 — January 9, 2026 — Iranian street protests grow amid an internet blackout**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, first published January 9 — [protest and crackdown reporting](https://www.theguardian.com/global-development/2026/jan/09/iran-supreme-leader-harsher-crackdown-protest-movement-swells).  
**Report:** Demonstrators took to Iranian streets as an economic protest movement widened into demands for political change. Authorities restricted internet access, and the supreme leader denounced protesters while reports emerged of lethal state repression.  
**Assessment:** **Protests and communications restrictions were reported contemporaneously.** Casualty totals, accusations that foreign governments directed the unrest, and individual responsibility remained subjects requiring independent verification.

**HN-1014 — January 14, 2026 — Greenland's future becomes a direct US–Denmark diplomatic dispute**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, January 14 — [report after Denmark–Greenland–US talks](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/jan/14/greenland-us-trump-talks-denmark).  
**Report:** The US president continued to argue that Greenland was indispensable to American security after talks with Danish and Greenlandic officials failed to bridge a fundamental disagreement about the territory's political future.  
**Assessment:** **A diplomatic confrontation, not a completed annexation or negotiated transfer.** Public threats and strategic assertions do not settle Greenlanders' political preferences or sovereignty questions.

**HN-1015 — January 24, 2026 — Footage challenges early claims about a second Minneapolis killing**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, January 24 — [Alex Pretti video review](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/jan/24/minneapolis-shooting-ice).  
**Report:** Federal agents fatally shot Alex Pretti in Minneapolis. The paper reviewed video showing him holding a telephone during a confrontation, contradicting an immediate characterization by President Trump that he was a gunman.  
**Assessment:** **The video is direct evidence about what was visible at particular moments, not a substitute for the entire investigative record.** Initial assertions about a weapon, threat and justification require comparison with all footage, physical evidence and subsequent proceedings.

**HN-1016 — February 3, 2026 — Fuel leaks postpone NASA's planned crewed Moon flyby**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, February 3 — [Artemis II launch-delay dispatch](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/feb/03/nasa-delays-moon-rocket-launch-month-fuel-leaks-artemis).  
**Report:** NASA postponed its projected Artemis II launch window after a test of the Space Launch System encountered hydrogen-fueling problems. Four astronauts waiting for a flight around the Moon would remain on Earth while engineers investigated.  
**Assessment:** **A schedule slip and engineering test problem, not a failed crewed mission.** Plans cited in February are distinguished from the April launch and return documented below (HN-1024 and HN-1026).

**HN-1017 — February 22, 2026 — Mexico confirms killing of cartel leader El Mencho**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, February 22 — [military operation and retaliation report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/feb/22/mexican-security-forces-reportedly-kill-drug-cartel-boss-el-mencho).  
**Report:** Mexico's defense ministry announced that security forces had killed Nemesio Oseguera Cervantes, known as El Mencho, after an operation targeting the Jalisco New Generation Cartel leader. Burning vehicles and armed roadblocks were reported across several states.  
**Assessment:** **The death was officially confirmed, while the extent of retaliatory violence was developing.** Early rumors about who controlled individual attacks and the final casualty total cannot be established from the first day's reports.

**HN-1018 — February 27, 2026 — Pakistan declares 'open war' as strikes hit Afghanistan**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, February 27 — [cross-border conflict report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/feb/27/pakistan-declares-state-of-open-war-after-bombing-major-afghan-cities).  
**Report:** Pakistani authorities described the confrontation with Taliban-ruled Afghanistan as open war after airstrikes on major Afghan cities followed attacks near their disputed and heavily militarized frontier.  
**Assessment:** **The public declaration and military escalation were reported; the phrase 'open war' was a government characterization.** Each side's accounts of casualties, targets and responsibility needed separate verification.

**HN-1019 — February 28, 2026 — US and Israel begin large-scale strikes on Iran**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, February 28 — [early Tehran strikes and leadership-claims report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/feb/28/israel-launches-attack-on-iran-as-explosions-heard-in-tehran).  
**Report:** American and Israeli forces attacked targets in Iran, with explosions reported in Tehran. As the conflict opened, leaders and media outlets issued rapidly changing accounts about the fate of senior Iranian officials.  
**Assessment:** **The outbreak of attacks was established; initial claims about individual leaders' deaths, the legality of the attacks and military success were not final verified findings.** Later ceasefire proposals did not retroactively resolve those questions.

**HN-1020 — March 3, 2026 — Strait of Hormuz traffic plunges during the Iran war**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, March 3 — [oil and shipping disruption report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/mar/03/iran-has-largely-halted-oil-and-gas-exports-through-strait-of-hormuz).  
**Report:** War-related threats and attacks sharply reduced oil and gas transport through the Strait of Hormuz, a crucial maritime passage for global energy supplies. Traders and governments confronted sudden disruption and uncertainty.  
**Assessment:** **A severe interruption in maritime exports is not evidence that every ship was stopped permanently.** Contemporary estimates of traffic, prices and responsibility reflect the reporting moment.

**HN-1021 — March 16, 2026 — Cuba's national electrical system fails amid fuel shortages**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, March 16 — [nationwide power-collapse report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/mar/16/cuba-electrical-grid-collapses-amid-us-oil-blockade).  
**Report:** Cuba experienced another countrywide grid failure while struggling to obtain fuel and maintain its electricity system amid US restrictions on oil supplies. Residents and essential services faced continuing outages.  
**Assessment:** **A documented blackout, not permanent extinction of the country's power grid.** The scale of outages and restoration must be tracked across later reports; a single cause should not be inferred from a multi-factor infrastructure crisis.

**HN-1022 — March 19, 2026 — New Monte Verde dating study reignites a prehistory dispute**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, March 19 — [Monte Verde archaeological challenge](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/mar/19/archaeological-site-in-chile-upends-theory-of-how-humans-populated-the-americas-again); *Science*/USGS, March 19 — [underlying re-dating study](https://www.usgs.gov/publications/a-mid-holocene-age-monte-verde-challenges-timeline-human-colonization-south-america).  
**Report:** Researchers argued that a famous archaeological site in Chile, long dated to approximately 14,500 years ago, may instead be 4,200–8,200 years old based on new sediment and geologic evidence. Their claim challenged one influential piece of evidence about early settlement in the Americas.  
**Assessment:** **A published challenge to the site's chronology, not an archaeological consensus suddenly overturned.** Other researchers dispute the interpretation, and the paper did not establish the first arrival date of all humans in the Americas.

**HN-1023 — March 20, 2026 — NASA rolls Artemis II back to its launch pad**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, March 20 — [rocket rollout and April launch target](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/mar/20/nasa-returns-moon-rocket-to-pad-targets-april-launch).  
**Report:** Engineers returned the Moon rocket to the launch pad and NASA set its sights on an April 1 crewed mission following delays and tests earlier in the year.  
**Assessment:** **A launch target and physical preparation, not a completed flight.** The actual launch and splashdown belong to separate later newspaper records.

**HN-1024 — April 1, 2026 — Artemis II lifts off with four astronauts**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, April 1 — [crewed lunar flyby launch report](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/apr/01/nasa-rocket-moon-launch-artemis-ii).  
**Report:** NASA launched its Artemis II astronauts in the Orion spacecraft on a journey around the Moon, the first crewed lunar voyage in more than half a century.  
**Assessment:** **A successfully reported launch, not a landing on the lunar surface or a completed round trip on April 1.** Mission return is independently documented in HN-1026.

**HN-1025 — April 7, 2026 — Washington and Tehran announce a provisional pause in fighting**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, April 7 — [US–Iran temporary ceasefire announcement](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/apr/07/trump-iran-war-ceasefire).  
**Report:** The United States and Iran announced a provisional ceasefire arrangement involving the Strait of Hormuz, creating an opening for further negotiations after weeks of war.  
**Assessment:** **A conditional diplomatic arrangement, not a comprehensive and permanently implemented peace treaty.** Statements about reopening the strait and the scope of the ceasefire were claims or commitments requiring later monitoring.

**HN-1026 — April 10, 2026 — Artemis II crew returns after journey around Moon**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, April 10 — [capsule splashdown and mission report](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/apr/10/artemis-ii-landing-return-moon-mission).  
**Report:** The astronauts aboard Artemis II splashed down after a lunar flyby, completing the flight that had been delayed during earlier ground testing.  
**Assessment:** **Successful crew return, distinct from launch or lunar landing.** Claims about all experimental findings and future Artemis schedules extend beyond the splashdown report.

**HN-1027 — April 12, 2026 — Orbán concedes defeat after 16 years governing Hungary**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, April 12 — [Hungarian election-night report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/apr/12/viktor-orban-concedes-defeat-as-opposition-wins-hungarian-election).  
**Report:** Prime minister Viktor Orbán conceded defeat after Hungary's opposition Tisza party, led by Péter Magyar, won the parliamentary election, marking a dramatic reversal after 16 years of Orbán's rule.  
**Assessment:** **An election outcome and concession, not a statement about the new administration's eventual policy achievements.** Final electoral certification and transfer of office are distinct procedural stages.

**HN-1028 — April 15, 2026 — Survey reveals layers of ancient shipwrecks near Gibraltar**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, April 15 — [underwater archaeology investigation](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/apr/15/hidden-treasures-spanish-archaeologists-discover-trove-of-ancient-shipwrecks-in-bay-of-gibraltar).  
**Report:** Spanish underwater archaeologists described a concentration of wreck sites in the Bay of Gibraltar spanning ancient to modern maritime commerce, with artefacts suggesting changing trade, naval and everyday life across centuries.  
**Assessment:** **A survey of multiple sites, not the discovery of a single intact treasure galleon.** Dates and identifications assigned to particular objects remain archaeological interpretations.

**HN-1029 — May 1, 2026 — Roman gravestone found in Louisiana yard goes home to Italy**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, May 1 — [repatriation of missing ancient stone](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/may/01/roman-gravestone-new-orleans-returned-italy).  
**Report:** An ancient Roman gravestone found in the backyard of a New Orleans property was returned to Italy through an FBI-assisted handover after researchers traced it to a museum collection disrupted during the second world war.  
**Assessment:** **The object's recovery and return were reported; its path through private hands was not fully reconstructed.** Finding a Roman stone in Louisiana does not mean a Roman settlement existed there.

**HN-1030 — May 14, 2026 — Cuban energy minister says diesel and fuel oil have run out**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, May 14 — [Cuban minister's fuel-supply warning](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/may/14/cuba-us-energy-blockade-oil-fuel-petrol-runs-out).  
**Report:** Cuba's energy minister said the country had exhausted supplies of diesel and fuel oil as US oil restrictions aggravated a prolonged energy emergency. Officials and residents faced further threats to generation, transport and daily life.  
**Assessment:** **An attributed government statement, not an independent physical audit showing that every type of fuel was unavailable everywhere.** The report followed, rather than replaced, earlier nationwide blackouts.

**HN-1031 — May 26, 2026 — Iran says peace negotiations continue despite fresh US bombing**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, May 26 — [renewed attacks and diplomatic claims](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/may/26/iran-peace-deal-talks-us-bombing-ceasefire-strait-hormuz).  
**Report:** Iranian officials described continuing peace contacts while denouncing US strikes on Iranian targets, showing the fragility of the April ceasefire and unsettled negotiations over security and shipping.  
**Assessment:** **Talks reportedly in progress, not a signed, implemented or durable peace deal.** Each government's description of compliance and the targets struck remained contested.

**HN-1032 — June 9, 2026 — Offshore Cuban earthquake shakes southern Florida**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 9 — [6.1-magnitude earthquake report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/jun/09/florida-earthquake-cuba-coast-mexico).  
**Report:** An earthquake reported at magnitude 6.1 struck offshore near Cuba and was felt across parts of the region, including southern Florida, setting off public inquiries about its strength and effects.  
**Assessment:** **An observed seismic event; the reported magnitude is a contemporaneous estimate.** Shaking felt at distant locations is not evidence of identical damage or intensity there.

**HN-1033 — June 18, 2026 — Two lost postholes point toward Stonehenge's solar origins**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 18 (published June 17 in US time zones) — [Bulford solstice-monument discovery](https://www.theguardian.com/uk-news/2026/jun/18/solstice-aligned-monument-archaeology-wiltshire-stonehenge-prototype).  
**Report:** Archaeologists identified two ancient postholes about 120 metres apart at Bulford, near Stonehenge. Radiocarbon evidence and reconstruction suggested that a pair of wooden uprights was aligned with the summer sunrise and winter sunset roughly 5,000 years ago.  
**Assessment:** **A physical excavation and evidence-based solstice alignment; the suggestion that this was a direct prototype for Stonehenge is interpretive.** The postholes do not disclose the builders' precise rituals or beliefs.

**HN-1034 — June 22, 2026 — Interstellar comet 3I/ATLAS may predate our solar system by billions of years**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 22 — [isotope-based comet-age report](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/jun/22/interstellar-comet-may-be-oldest-object-seen-in-our-solar-system-research-finds).  
**Report:** Scientists studying unusual isotope ratios in comet 3I/ATLAS proposed that it could be as old as 12 billion years, compared with the solar system's roughly 4.5 billion years.  
**Assessment:** **A scientific age estimate, not a directly measured birth date or proof of the oldest object ever observed.** The research team acknowledged other explanations for the comet's chemistry; an alien-craft theory was not supported by these observations.

**HN-1035 — June 24, 2026 — Perseverance finds complex organic carbon in Martian rocks**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, June 24 — [Sherloc instrument and Bright Angel rocks report](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/jun/24/nasa-rover-detects-potential-signatures-ancient-microbial-life-mars).  
**Report:** NASA's Perseverance rover detected macromolecular carbon in ancient Martian mudstones along a former river channel, adding chemical evidence to rocks previously noted for intriguing textures.  
**Assessment:** **Organic compounds and potentially life-compatible conditions were detected; ancient life was not confirmed.** Nonbiological geological and impact processes can produce the same broad category of material.

**HN-1036 — July 5, 2026 — Severe US heat brings preliminary reports of 25 deaths**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, July 5 — [record heatwave and fatalities report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/jul/05/heatwave-deaths-weather).  
**Report:** A widespread heatwave brought unusually high temperatures to multiple US regions as local authorities and reporters attributed at least 25 deaths to the oppressive conditions.  
**Assessment:** **A contemporaneous cross-jurisdiction count, not a final certified measure of heat-attributable mortality.** Death-certificate review and indirect excess-death estimates may produce different totals.

**HN-1037 — July 19, 2026 — Spain wins the World Cup after extra-time final against Argentina**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, match July 19; report dated July 20 in central Europe — [Spain–Argentina final match account](https://www.theguardian.com/football/2026/jul/19/spain-win-world-cup-argentina-final-match-report).  
**Report:** Spain defeated Argentina 1–0 after extra time in the men's football World Cup final in New Jersey. Substitute Ferran Torres scored in the 106th minute against an Argentine team reduced to ten men.  
**Assessment:** **A recorded final result, separate from journalists' evaluative judgments about how well the teams played.** The date discrepancy reflects local kickoff and article publication time zones.

**HN-1038 — July 28, 2026 — Magnitude-6.8 quake hits southern Japan**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, late July 28 US time / July 29 Japan — [Kyushu earthquake rescue report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/jul/29/japan-earthquake-death-aftershocks).  
**Report:** A strong earthquake struck Japan's Kyushu island, disrupting water and power supplies and prompting a major search operation. The paper reported at least 13 deaths during early rescue work and a maximum 7 on Japan's intensity scale.  
**Assessment:** **The quake and disruption were documented, but the casualty number was provisional.** Magnitude 6.8 and Japanese seismic *intensity* 7 are different measurements; they should not be conflated.

**HN-1039 — August 1, 2026 — Seismic swarm injures dozens near Naples**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, August 1 — [earthquake and injury report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/aug/01/scores-injured-as-earthquake-hits-near-naples-in-seismic-swarm).  
**Report:** Tremors in a seismic swarm near Naples, Italy, damaged buildings and injured residents as authorities responded to the latest episode of unrest in an active volcanic region.  
**Assessment:** **Recorded earthquake activity is not the same as a volcanic eruption.** Injury figures and damage assessments remained preliminary.

**HN-1040 — August 10, 2026 — Typhoon Dolphin drives evacuations in China**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, August 10 — [Shanghai rainfall and evacuation coverage](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/aug/10/typhoon-dolphin-china-shanghai-flooding-evacuations).  
**Report:** Chinese authorities moved more than one million people away from danger as Typhoon Dolphin produced torrential rain and severe weather around Shanghai and eastern China.  
**Assessment:** **Large evacuations and rainfall were reported, but early evacuation and loss figures can change.** The article's description of record precipitation is tied to particular stations, periods or localities rather than all of China.

**HN-1041 — August 10, 2026 — Roman cargo ship with hundreds of jars located off Sicily**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, August 10 — [Mazara del Vallo amphora wreck report](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/aug/10/roman-shipwreck-with-500-amphorae-found-off-coast-of-sicily).  
**Report:** Divers working with Italy's cultural-heritage authorities located a roughly 2,100-year-old vessel near Sicily at about 46 metres depth, with approximately 500 amphorae, many of a type commonly used to transport wine.  
**Assessment:** **A located wreck and observed cargo; the exact route, ownership, destination and reasons for sinking had not yet been established.** The amphora count is an approximate first assessment.

**HN-1042 — August 12, 2026 — Total solar eclipse draws crowds across Europe**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, August 12 — [post-eclipse accounts across Europe](https://www.theguardian.com/science/ng-interactive/2026/aug/12/cruise-ships-quiet-towns-and-an-all-day-festival-millions-flock-to-eclipse-viewing-spots-across-europe).  
**Report:** Observers in Greenland, Iceland, parts of Spain and Portugal saw the Sun completely obscured by the Moon, while millions more across Europe witnessed varying partial phases. Travel companies, scientists and villages organized elaborate viewing events.  
**Assessment:** **A directly observed eclipse.** Totality occurred only inside its narrow path; crowd forecasts were not necessarily final attendance counts, and partial viewing elsewhere was not total darkness.

**HN-1043 — August 12, 2026 — Astronomers propose a new 'black hole star' category**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, August 12 — [James Webb astronomical interpretation](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/aug/12/astronomy-discovery-new-cosmic-object-black-hole-star).  
**Report:** Astronomers described observations they argued might indicate a black hole enveloped by dense material that resembles a star, offering a possible explanation for mysterious compact red objects seen by the James Webb telescope.  
**Assessment:** **A proposed interpretation of observations, not confirmed discovery of an entirely new established class of stellar object.** Alternatives remain under investigation.

**HN-1044 — August 30, 2026 — NASA's Roman Space Telescope begins its mission**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, launch coverage published August 29–30 across time zones — [Nancy Grace Roman launch report](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/aug/29/nasa-telescope-launch-nancy-grace-roman).  
**Report:** A Falcon Heavy rocket launched NASA's Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope, designed for wide-field space surveys relevant to dark energy, exoplanets and galaxy formation. The mission proceeded after earlier proposals to cancel or cut the program were rejected.  
**Assessment:** **A confirmed launch, not proof that the telescope had already delivered its planned years of observations.** Budget proposals should not be mistaken for enacted termination of the mission.

**HN-1045 — September 3, 2026 — Ig Nobel ceremony leaves the United States for the first time**  
**Source:** *Associated Press* syndicated contemporary report, September 3 — [cockroach-milk and nose-blowing prize coverage](https://www.wcax.com/2026/09/03/scientists-studying-cockroach-milk-nose-blowing-win-ig-nobel-prizes-quirky-science/).  
**Report:** Researchers studying unusual subjects such as cockroach milk and nose-blowing received Ig Nobel prizes at a ceremony in Zurich. Organizers held the irreverent awards outside the US for the first time after expressing concerns about international travel and visas.  
**Assessment:** **These were satirical Ig Nobel prizes, which are separate from the official Nobel awards.** The organizers' stated reasons for relocating the event are attributed explanations, not a general finding about every researcher's travel prospects.

**HN-1046 — September 9, 2026 — Ancient Indonesian teeth hint at use of psychoactive nuts**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, September 9 — [prehistoric tooth-wear and residue study](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/sep/09/indonesian-foragers-mind-altering-nuts-research-finds).  
**Report:** Analysis of teeth from two ancient Indonesian skeletons suggested that people roughly 25,000 years ago may have chewed or sucked nuts containing psychoactive compounds, leaving unusual wear and chemical traces.  
**Assessment:** **Material evidence and a hypothesis about its cause, not a reliable description of the individuals' intent or proof of recreational drug use.** Toothache remedies and alternative explanations remain possible.

**HN-1047 — September 16, 2026 — Newly reported lunar crater links modern images to a 2024 impact**  
**Source:** *Associated Press*, September 16 — [new Moon crater report](https://ny1.com/nyc/all-boroughs/ap-top-news/2026/09/16/nasa-spacecraft-discovers-a-huge-new-crater-on-the-moon); NASA, September 16 — [agency findings summarized via published release](https://ebs.publicnow.com/view/CB2C577F9EF074E0F87EEB49B908F4AD3E7EA4F5).  
**Report:** NASA scientists described an unusually large, newly formed lunar impact crater found in Lunar Reconnaissance Orbiter images. Comparing observations dated the collision to April–May 2024, although its scientific announcement came in September 2026.  
**Assessment:** **Discovery announcement date and formation date are distinct.** The crater was not formed in September 2026; size and impactor estimates rest on orbital measurements and models.

**HN-1048 — September 17, 2026 — Astronomers investigate why feeding black holes 'burp'**
