**Source:** *The Guardian*, September 17 — [black-hole accretion and eruption research](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/sep/17/black-hole-burps-scientists-solve-mystery-astronomy).  
**Report:** Researchers proposed an explanation for delayed eruptions and radio emission associated with black holes consuming nearby material, building a more detailed picture of how disrupted matter behaves.  
**Assessment:** **Scientific modeling and observed signatures, not a final explanation for every supermassive black hole.** The newspaper's colorful term 'burp' describes astrophysical emission, not sound traveling through space.

**HN-1049 — September 23, 2026 — Ancient wall beneath Paris offers clue to Lutetia's origins**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, September 23 — [archaeological wall near Notre-Dame](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/sep/23/ancient-wall-paris-first-settlement-archaeology-roman).  
**Report:** Archaeologists uncovered about 20 metres of a Gallo-Roman-period wall beneath the Île de la Cité near Notre-Dame. French officials suggested it might identify part of the earliest defended settlement of Lutetia, predecessor of Paris.  
**Assessment:** **A verified excavated structure, but its association with the city's first settlement is a proposed identification.** The discovery does not, by itself, establish an exact foundation date for Paris.

**HN-1050 — September 25, 2026 — Microbes endure laboratory conditions resembling Enceladus's ocean**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, September 25 — [organisms in simulated Saturn-moon conditions](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/sep/25/earth-microbes-survive-conditions-enceladus-saturn-moon-ocean-study).  
**Report:** Scientists found that some Earth microorganisms could survive experiments designed to replicate aspects of the subsurface ocean thought to lie beneath Enceladus's icy crust, sharpening scientific interest in its habitability.  
**Assessment:** **A laboratory survival result, not detection of life on Enceladus.** The actual moon's chemistry, energy availability and long-term conditions remain partly inferred from remote observations.

**HN-1051 — October 5, 2026 — Medicine Nobel recognizes optogenetics pioneers**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, October 5 — [2026 physiology-or-medicine prize announcement](https://www.theguardian.com/science/2026/oct/05/nobel-prize-medicine-2026-winner); Nobel-linked university report — [Stanford's recognition of Karl Deisseroth](https://news.stanford.edu/stories/2026/10/karl-deisseroth-nobel-prize-physiology-medicine).  
**Report:** The Nobel committee awarded the medicine prize to Karl Deisseroth, Peter Hegemann and Georg Nagel for discoveries enabling optogenetics, the technique of controlling selected neurons with light.  
**Assessment:** **An official prize decision recognizing a research method, not a claim that scientists can now read every human thought or cure every neurological disorder.** The award is distinct from the September Ig Nobel ceremony.

**HN-1052 — October 17, 2026 — French protests turn violent amid a cost-of-living conflict**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, first published October 17 — [police clashes and arrests report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/oct/17/protests-france-high-prices-social-security-gilets-jaunes).  
**Report:** Widespread demonstrations in France over prices and social protections were accompanied by clashes, fires and a police announcement of more than 1,800 arrests.  
**Assessment:** **An unfolding protest and policing record.** The reported arrest total is not a conviction tally; neither isolated acts of destruction nor the police response can automatically be attributed to everyone participating in the demonstrations.

**HN-1053 — October 19, 2026 — Hurricane Trudy threatens Mexico as floods strike Greece**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, October 19 — [weather-tracker report across two regions](https://www.theguardian.com/news/2026/oct/19/hurricane-trudy-mexico-pacific-coast-greece-floods).  
**Report:** Forecasters tracked Trudy near Mexico's Pacific coast while another severe rain system caused damaging floods and a reported death in Greece. The paper combined two geographically independent weather emergencies.  
**Assessment:** **Forecast hurricane threats should not be represented as final observed Mexican damage.** The Greece floods were a separate event, not a consequence of the Pacific storm, and loss figures were early reports.

**HN-1054 — October 21, 2026 — Scholar advances an answer to Westminster Abbey sarcophagus mystery**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, October 21 — [historical burial identification claim](https://www.theguardian.com/uk-news/2026/oct/21/mystery-roman-sarcophagus-westminster-abbey-solved-at-last-expert-says).  
**Report:** A Westminster Abbey archaeologist proposed that remains found long ago in a reused Roman sarcophagus were those of a medieval abbot, offering a possible solution to a puzzle dating back to nineteenth-century excavations.  
**Assessment:** **An expert's argued identification, not automatically a conclusive DNA or documentary match.** The Roman age of the stone coffin does not make its later occupant a Roman-era burial.

**HN-1055 — October 21, 2026 — US Supreme Court temporarily blocks a transgender-prisoner care restriction**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, October 21 — [interim court order report](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/oct/21/supreme-court-trump-gender-affirming-care-trans-prisoners).  
**Report:** The US Supreme Court halted, at least temporarily, a Trump administration restriction affecting gender-affirming healthcare for transgender federal prisoners while the legal dispute proceeded.  
**Assessment:** **An interim judicial action, not necessarily a final ruling on the merits or nationwide resolution of all prison medical-care disputes.** The exact order and procedural posture control its scope.

**HN-1056 — October 22, 2026 — Iran warns of attacks on Gulf oil and AI facilities if war resumes**  
**Source:** *The Guardian*, October 22 — [conditional threat and regional security report](https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/oct/22/iran-threatens-seven-gulf-countries-us-israel-renew-attacks).  
**Report:** Iranian authorities threatened facilities across seven Gulf countries, including oil and artificial-intelligence infrastructure, if the US and Israel renewed attacks. The warning emerged amid an unstable regional truce and renewed fears for energy trade.  
**Assessment:** **A reported conditional threat, not evidence that those facilities had already been attacked or destroyed.** The ledger stops on this date; subsequent developments and statements are outside this pass.

## Search coverage and important limits

1. **1777–1785:** Library of Congress date filters indicated **10,602 indexed page positions**. An index retrieval using two pagination orderings yielded **10,599 distinct page identifiers**, leaving a discrepancy of **three**.
2. **1786–1795:** The LOC date filters indicated **14,134 indexed page positions**. We retrieved **13,435 distinct page records** (about 95% of the indicated total), leaving an index-position discrepancy of **699**. Most missing positions fall in **1795**, when the archive repeatedly returned HTTP 429 rate limits. The range is **not fully indexed**, and this does not include all newspapers ever printed.
3. **1796–1805:** The LOC date filters indicated approximately **24,945 indexed page positions** across the ten years. This initial pass searched only **about 2,080 indexed page previews**, mostly the earliest dates in each year; the LOC began returning **HTTP 429 rate limits** during collection. This date range is therefore **substantially incomplete**, and the 24,945 positions are **not** pages reviewed. The period coverage is not a census of all newspapers published.
4. **1806–1815:** The LOC collection endpoint continued returning **HTTP 429**, so we **did not complete a new LOC decade index**. We used **selected full-text searches** in *Western Sun* and *Western Eagle* at Hoosier State Chronicles, plus the **four pages of the July 24, 1806** issue of *The Statesman* from the British Library's openly downloadable ALTO OCR. These are targeted samples from certain newspapers, **not comprehensive year-by-year coverage**. One Indiana search for the word “murder” returned 59 matching pages in the date range; that is a search-hit count, not a count of all newspaper pages or distinct incidents.
5. **1816–1825:** Initial keyword searches in the Hoosier State Chronicles digitized Indiana newspapers yielded candidate page indexes spanning the decade (e.g. **492 page hits for “murder,” 40 for “mutiny,” and 38 for “forgery”**). We selected and read **individual full-page OCR transcriptions** and several separately filtered 1816 and 1818 search results. These results include reprints of foreign and other U.S. reports, not a census of events. Other searches sometimes returned **HTTP 520** and the LOC endpoint was not comprehensively indexed for this range. **Most newspaper pages and titles were not reviewed**; hit counts do not equal distinct incidents.
6. **1826–1835:** An initial set of Hoosier State Chronicles keyword searches returned candidate page counts including **1,396 hits for “murder,” 546 for “fraud,” 141 for “forgery,” and 119 for “mutiny”** across the decade. These are **search hits**, not pages fully reviewed or unique incidents. We selected and read full OCR text from a few dozen original pages, including reprints of other states' and foreign news, and noted when a matching word occurred in fiction rather than an actual report. Some follow-up searches encountered **HTTP 520** responses. This is a **small, nonrepresentative sample**, not an exhaustive review; **no new discovery was selected for publication year 1828** in this pass.
7. **1836–1845:** Targeted Hoosier State Chronicles keyword searches initially returned indexes of **1,345 page hits for “murder,” 472 for “poison,” 250 for “forgery,” 123 for “mutiny,” 357 for “robbery,” 417 for “explosion,” and 518 for “mysterious”** across the decade. These are **search-result page counts only**, not fully inspected pages, distinct articles, or unique real-world events. About two dozen selected page OCR transcriptions were fetched and examined for this addition, including year-specific 1840 searches and 1843 follow-up on the *Somers*. The Indiana archive intermittently returned **HTTP 520**, and some initially selected 1840 pages could not be fetched. The selected pages reproduce reports from New York, Philadelphia, Missouri, Louisiana, and elsewhere; this is **not** a representative geographic sample. Some matching text was fiction, editorial invective, or ordinary metaphor, rather than a news discovery.
8. **1846–1855:** Targeted Hoosier State Chronicles searches for **murder (2,886 matching pages), poison (1,727), forgery (476), mutiny (149), robbery (692), mysterious (995), and unusual (2,217)** generated candidates across the decade. These figures come from keyword-search results; they are **not distinct stories or pages checked**. Separate focused searches were run for 1851 and 1853. Only selected original-page OCR transcriptions were read, with several **HTTP 520** errors during retrieval or follow-up searches. The selection is strongly biased toward accessible digitized Indiana papers, many carrying reprints of events elsewhere. **It is not a comprehensive search of any year or a national event census.**
9. **1856–1865:** Targeted Hoosier State Chronicles keyword searches returned candidate pages for **1856–1860**, including murder (**3,241**), mutiny (**212**), explosion (**721**), ghost (**422**), forgery (**359**), and fraud (**2,541**). Searches for **1861–1865** returned murder (**1,887**), mutiny (**214**), explosion (**684**), spy (**1,086**), balloon (**229**), and assassination (**400**); focused 1864 searches returned torpedo (**64**) and submarine (**11**). These figures are **keyword-matching page results, not unique stories or pages read**; a single page may appear under multiple terms. We examined **selected original-page OCR transcriptions** from each year, not every result. Some search and document requests failed with **HTTP 520**. The underlying digitized Indiana papers frequently reproduce national and foreign news. This is a **small, nonrepresentative discovery sample**, not a comprehensive survey of wartime or postwar newspapers.
10. **1866–1875:** Targeted Hoosier State Chronicles keyword searches produced candidate page-hit totals including **mysterious (3,080), mutiny (348), explosion (4,473), fraud (5,391), poison (6,776), ghost (1,820), and forgery (1,428)**; a broad “murder” request failed with **HTTP 520**. Focused 1866–1870 searches returned **mysterious disappearance (121), train robbery (1,218), murder trial (3,812), shipwreck (121), and strange death (2,800)**; focused 1871–1875 searches included **murder trial (3,618), train wreck (1,008), shipwreck (165), and poisoning (583)**. Other queries failed with **HTTP 520**. These figures mean **keyword-matching page results**, never unique incidents, confirmed facts, or pages read. We examined a few dozen selected original-page OCR transcriptions and several contextual sources, not the full result sets. Some purported “ghosts” were jokes, and some searches matched fiction, political rhetoric, or OCR collisions. Indiana papers regularly reprinted national and international stories. The sample is **geographically and topically biased, incomplete, and not a comprehensive account of any year**. Even some apparent dates and place names require checking against page images or other originals.
11. **1876–1885:** An initial Hoosier State Chronicles keyword pass returned **5,423 page hits for “mysterious,” 506 for “mutiny,” 7,757 for “explosion,” 9,809 for “fraud,” 9,181 for “poison,” 2,589 for “ghost,” 2,899 for “forgery,” and 17,765 for “murder.”** Narrower **1876–1880** queries returned **bank robbery (1,107), poisoning (750), and lynched (238)**. In **1881–1885** the selected search counts included **dynamite (3,381), poisoning (2,091), bank failure (7,079), murder trial (8,384), lynching (1,335), and shipwreck (257)**; a one-year **1881** scan additionally gave **explosion (612), mysterious (435), robbery (483), railroad disaster (286), murder (1,589), and fire (3,530)**. Several phrase searches and individual page requests failed with **HTTP 520**. These counts describe **keyword-matching pages returned by an index, not pages completely read, distinct incidents, or confirmed facts**. We selected and inspected several dozen original-page OCR texts, along with the **1861** contemporary newspaper obituary that contradicts a **1877** reprint and a **City of Vienna** retrospective on the 1881 Ringtheater fire. Numerous hits were fictional stories, historical retrospectives, metaphors, advertisements, or partisan commentary and were excluded from contemporaneous-crime or disaster entries. The collection mainly represents **Indiana newspapers reprinting much wider geographical news**: this remains a **selective, non-exhaustive, nonrepresentative discovery pass**.
12. **1886–1895:** Targeted Hoosier State Chronicles keyword searches returned results including **bank robbery (7,441 matching pages), mysterious disappearance (1,272), lynched (4,286), mutiny (983), dynamite (9,724), forgery (6,044), poisoning (7,229), train wreck (15,383), ghost (4,808), shipwreck (837), fraud (13,348), and explosion (17,980)**. These numbers are **search-hit counts, not pages read or distinct incidents**; many pages match several keywords and many hits are editorials, fiction, religion, advertisements, or historical retrospectives. We examined **dozens of selected full-page OCR transcriptions**, not every hit; some primary-page requests returned **HTTP 520** and are excluded from factual accounts unless an independently retrieved original was examined. The selected Indiana newspapers carry reprinted stories from all over the United States and Europe; their coverage is **narrowly selected, regionally biased, and far from exhaustive**. Verification included the **Kansas Historical Society** on the **1888 Frontenac** death toll (HN-277); a **federal mine-safety technical history** on **1891 Mammoth** (HN-286); a Washington historical study of mine-inspection and inquest records for **1892 Roslyn** (HN-290); and **Ida B. Wells's 1895 *A Red Record*** correcting the geography and one-victim framing of a **1893 Quincy lynching** reprint (HN-295). The original sources themselves often contain incompatible casualty estimates, cause hypotheses, and sometimes incorrect datelines. We preserved these as **reported uncertainty** rather than treating every printed claim as true.
13. Broad discovery searches before 1806 ran across the LOC API's **short OCR descriptions/previews** for retrieved pages. The previews are only a fraction of each page's text. Thus none of the periods represents a page-by-page full-text exhaustive review.
14. Selected primary pages were then retrieved through the LOC's **full-page OCR text service**, the Indiana collection's full-text page views, and the British Library's OCR files. OCR errors remain possible, especially where columns interleave.
15. Later coverage, court records, logbooks, and secondary histories have **not** been systematically checked for every entry. **“Follow-up open” means unresearched, never “it vanished” or “it was suppressed.”** Some follow-up is explicitly included, especially HN-085, HN-139–HN-141, HN-160, and HN-170/HN-173/HN-174. For HN-196 and HN-197, the later battle outcome and a prompt official correction respectively are linked to avoid perpetuating incomplete or false first dispatches. In the 1866–1875 addition, we explicitly retain contradictory reports of the *Polaris* expedition (HN-222/HN-224), identify the Florida setting of an initially misleadingly datelined account (HN-209), distinguish Mumler's trial from a conviction (HN-213), correct the location of the *Schiller* wreck (HN-231), and pair the first *Mosel* explosion theory with a contemporary allegation of deliberate insurance fraud (HN-233). The 1876–1885 records additionally separate a **false 1877 death report** from an original **1861** obituary (HN-239), prevent a judicial finding of documentary forgery from being confused with a defendant's conviction (HN-248), compare witness versions of the Winston train robbery (HN-250), correct early Ringtheater casualty claims with Vienna's records (HN-253), and distinguish 1884 bank resumption from the following financial failure (HN-263). For 1886–1895, we distinguish **a missing person from an alleged murder** (HN-270/HN-272), **arrests from guilt and verdicts from later reversal** (HN-271/HN-276/HN-300), **false death counts from verified historical totals** (HN-277/HN-286/HN-290), **a demonstrably fictitious Rothschild rumor** (HN-285), **a 1893 newspaper reporting a late-1892 accident** (HN-292), and **Ida B. Wells's competing contemporary account of Quincy, Mississippi, instead of the reprint's erroneous Massachusetts dateline** (HN-295). Wells's wording and the reprint do not yet reconcile the year or exact number of people murdered; these remain open.

16. **1896–1905:** Targeted Hoosier State Chronicles searches returned indexed candidate page counts including **bank robbery (5,919)**, **poisoning (6,316)**, **strange disappearance (1,229)**, **mine explosion (6,684)**, **lynching (5,214)**, **train robbery (6,659)**, **hurricane (2,418)**, **mutiny (1,177)**, **plague (4,593)** and **fraud scheme (2,358)**. Some broad queries failed with **HTTP 520**. Narrower **1901** searches included **McKinley (4,251)** and **assassination (839)**; focused **1903** results included **Iroquois theater (39)** and **murder confession (408)**. These numbers are **keyword-matched page positions, not individual events, verified claims, or pages fully read**. We read **more than forty selected full-page/article OCR texts**, checking later records from the **U.S. Navy**, **National Park Service**, **state mine inspectors and archives**, **Library of Congress**, **CDC**, **Alberta Geological Survey**, **Carnegie Hero Fund**, **Stanford historians** and **Northwestern University's homicide records** where relevant. This is **not representative or exhaustive**: most accessible newspapers and indexed pages were not searched or read, and the Indiana sources frequently reprinted outside news. Sensational first accounts often got death totals, causes, or locations wrong. The 1898 plague announcement is **not a verified outbreak** (HN-316); the original article concerning **“Tea Poisoning”** in 1899 proved to be a **commercial bitters advertisement**, not a medical report, and was rejected. **“Follow-up open” never establishes concealment or a crime.** Significant corrected cases include **Red Ash (46 killed, not 125; HN-321)**, **Lost Creek (20, not 50; HN-330)**, **Hanna (169, not 234; HN-337)**, **Frank Slide (Alberta geological collapse, not a confirmed mine-gas blast in British Columbia; HN-336)**, **Iroquois Theatre (602; HN-340)**, and the independently documented **1905 Lima guilty pleas (HN-349)**. 

17. **1906–1915:** Targeted Hoosier State Chronicles searches yielded large pools of keyword-matched newspaper pages, including **mine explosion (2,786)**, **bank robbery (2,704)**, **lynching (1,071)**, **poisoning (4,438)**, **shipwreck (344)**, **mysterious disappearance (620)**, **train wreck (6,710)**, **aviator killed (1,495)**, **mutiny (813)**, **fraud scheme (533)**, **earthquake (3,311)**, and **conspiracy (4,922)**. These figures reflect **search results, not unique stories, pages individually read, or verified events**. Additional focused searches split the decade into smaller year ranges. We inspected selected original full-text OCR, and corroborated key events against the **USGS**, **National Park Service**, **Smithsonian**, **NIOSH/MSHA**, **Federal Reserve**, **National Weather Service**, **Library of Congress**, **U.S. Navy**, **Parks Canada**, and **U.S. Holocaust Memorial Museum**, among other institutional histories. Some original archive pages failed transiently and were not used as unsupported records. This is **not a comprehensive review of the period or of any locality**; many Indiana papers syndicated distant accounts. Particularly important corrections include **San Francisco's roughly 3,000 deaths versus an early 5,000 report** (HN-353), **Monongah's 362 rather than 500** (HN-359), **Cherry's 259 rather than 384** (HN-368), **Triangle's 146 rather than 141** (HN-376), **Dayton's 361 rather than claims of 5,000–10,000** (HN-384/HN-385), and ***Eastland*'s 844 rather than 1,500** (HN-397). Likewise, a 1909 North Pole item is **satire** (HN-367); allegations in racial violence, crime, and wartime accounts are not silently promoted to fact. Where later historical consensus is supplied, it does **not validate every contemporary eyewitness detail**.

18. **1916–1925:** Targeted searches in the Hoosier State Chronicles generated candidate-page pools including **fraud (5,356)**, **shipwreck (439)**, **mysterious murder (1,759)**, **race riot (2,390)**, **Wall Street explosion (1,651)**, **Blair Mountain (372)**, **Tri-State Tornado (225)**, and **Scopes trial (168)** across the decade, along with many year-filtered searches. These are **keyword hits, neither unique events nor records read**; broad phrases sometimes matched loosely, and some archive requests failed. **Selected original-page OCR** was inspected for the **46 new records HN-400–HN-445**, while later institutional histories and proceedings were checked where applicable. This remains **highly selective**, concentrated on Indiana newspapers often syndicating incidents elsewhere, and cannot be treated as exhaustive surveillance of the historical record. Corrections of particular importance include **Black Tom sabotage versus first-day uncertainty (HN-403)**; the ***Imo*, not the *Ionia*, in Halifax (HN-407)**; the **Army's 2023 vacatur of 110 Houston courts-martial (HN-406/HN-408)**; **Chicago's 38 deaths versus evolving tolls of 27 and 32 (HN-415/HN-416)**; **Newberry's later reversal (HN-419/HN-420/HN-425)**; **Knickerbocker's 98 versus an initial 107 (HN-430)**; **Great Kantō's approximately 105,000 dead or missing versus 250,000 claimed (HN-435)**; and **the Tri-State Tornado's 695 versus 1,000-plus initially reported (HN-441)**. Arrests, a prosecutor's allegations, and political propaganda are not recast as proven conspiracy; investigations and convictions are kept distinct from later reversals.

19. **1926–1935:** Searches in the Hoosier State Chronicles produced decade-spanning candidates, including **mysterious disappearance (429 matching pages)**, **massacre (965)**, **hurricane (1,378)**, **dynamite plot (269)**, and **train wreck (2,218)**, plus more narrowly date-filtered and incident-specific queries. These figures are **search hits, not distinct incidents, pages read, evidence of wrongdoing, or complete historical coverage**. The **53 selected records HN-446–HN-498** are based on **inspected original newspaper-page OCR**, with corroboration and corrections where available from the National Weather Service, National Archives, Federal Reserve, U.S. State Department, FBI, Ohio History Connection, Bath School Museum, *Indianapolis Recorder*, and other historical institutions. The archive is **mostly Indiana news**, much of it syndicating out-of-state and international dispatches. **Only a small portion of available originals was examined; this is not an exhaustive decade survey.** Particularly important contrasts are **McPherson's contested kidnapping versus a dismissed prosecution (HN-446/HN-447)**; **the 1927 flood's displacement rising beyond first figures (HN-451)**; **Bath's casualty-definition differences (HN-452/HN-453)**; **Okeechobee's at-least-2,500 dead versus the early estimates (HN-458/HN-459)**; **maritime casualty and arson claims (HN-460/HN-461/HN-490/HN-491)**; **the Ohio prison's 322 deaths versus an additional 150 feared dying (HN-466)**; **the Marion lynching victims versus untried allegations and ongoing investigation (HN-467/HN-469)**; **Mukden's contested railway pretext (HN-473/HN-475)**; **Utah's November anticipated result versus December formal ratification (HN-484/HN-485)**; and **the 1935 hurricane's feared 700 deaths rather than a certified total (HN-495–HN-497)**. The *Indianapolis Recorder* preserves an important Black press perspective on racial violence and unjust prosecutions, but even its reports of arrests or appeals do **not** substitute for completed court records. **Historical conclusions remain bounded by the sources actually examined**, and named open questions are not evidence of concealment.

20. **1936–1945:** Targeted, individually year-filtered searches of the **Hoosier State Chronicles** collection located candidate pages using incident names and terms involving disasters, war, political repression, disappearances, and later retrospective evidence. Additional focused searches sought **Jesse Owens**, the **1938 radio scare**, the **invasion of Poland and Soviet Union**, **Japanese American forced removal**, the **July 1944 Hitler assassination attempt**, and **1945 surrender and nuclear-war reporting**. **Search hits are not unique incidents or pages read**; only the **selected original-page OCR records** underlying **HN-499–HN-556 (58 new records)** were inspected, along with rejected candidates and original follow-ups. Institutional corroboration includes the **National Weather Service, National Archives, National Park Service, U.S. Navy, FBI, U.S. State Department, U.S. Holocaust Memorial Museum, Texas and Detroit historical institutions, NFPA, and Jewish/Black contemporary presses**. This is a **highly selective collection of mostly Indiana newspapers syndicating world news**, not a systematic national press review, a full WWII war chronology, or a complete war-crimes investigation. Key distinctions include **2,087 early heat deaths versus approximately 5,000 later (HN-500)**; ***Hindenburg* sabotage speculation and 32 missing/dead versus 36 deaths (HN-507)**; **Earhart radio rumor versus unsuccessful search (HN-508/HN-509)**; **the exaggerated Martian panic and Nazi pogrom arrest counts (HN-513–HN-515)**; **German *Athenia* disinformation versus documented *U-30* torpedoing (HN-518/HN-519)**; **enemy-alien exclusion versus Japanese American mass incarceration (HN-530/HN-531)**; **Cocoanut Grove's changing 478/474 and later 492 counts (HN-535/HN-536)**; **the Bari mustard-gas cargo unmentioned in first accounts (HN-541/HN-542)**; and **Hartford fire counts still varying among state sources (HN-544/HN-545)**. **Military communiqués, propaganda, partial casualty rosters, and columnist accusations are attributed rather than adopted as proved fact.** Some digitized OCR is imperfect (particularly older-font and crowded pages), so names, numbers, and purported original typographical errors may need manual examination of page images. Specific unresolved points are retained in the queue below.
21. **1946–1955:** Individually year-filtered original-text searches in **Hoosier State Chronicles** found candidate pages on **nuclear tests, breaking casualty totals, war and political conflict, scientific rumors, the polio epidemic, and civil-rights organizing**. Focused follow-up searches sought **the Berlin blockade/airlift, Woodward tornado, Palestine partition, Korean invasion, London smog, 1954 Japanese fishing-boat fallout, and Montgomery's bus boycott**. **Only the selected 53 page OCR texts underlying HN-557–HN-609, plus some rejected and contextual pages, were read**; an index search result was never itself counted as inspected primary reporting. Many sources are **Indiana syndication**, with selected ***Jewish Post* and *Indianapolis Recorder*** reporting widening the perspectives but not making the sample national or representative. Official/historical comparisons were drawn from **the U.S. Holocaust Memorial Museum, National Archives, National Park Service, NWS/NOAA, EPA, Department of Energy, U.S. State Department, U.S. Senate/House history offices, CDC, UN, Royal Household, Truman Library, Texas State Historical Association, and U.S. Fire Administration**. Critical corrections include **Winecoff's 122 versus documented 119 (HN-560)**; **Texas City's 700 versus the Texas memorial's 576 known dead, with uncertainty surviving (HN-562)**; **Donora's 18-to-20 revision (HN-571/HN-572)**; **the 1949 Soviet test date versus announcement date (HN-575)**; **Worcester's rising 82/89 preliminary counts (HN-593/HN-594)**; **three reported versus four documented 1954 Capitol assailants (HN-596)**; **Le Mans's preliminary 78 versus a commonly cited 84 total (HN-603)**; and **court verdicts separated from evidence of wrongdoing in the Till case (HN-605–HN-607)**. London-smog searches returned **no selected 1952 contemporary mortality report**, so its later-established toll has **not** been retrofitted as an independently read newspaper finding. Several official contextual links provide historical syntheses, **not complete original case files**; unresolved investigations remain below.
22. **1956–1965:** Individually year-scoped keyword searches of **Hoosier State Chronicles** identified candidate pages covering the **Montgomery boycott, Suez and Hungary, aviation disasters, Little Rock, influenza, early satellites, the Chicago school fire, Alaska statehood and tsunami, U-2 reconnaissance, Chile's earthquake, spaceflight, the Bay of Pigs, the Cuban Missile Crisis, the Kennedy assassination, the 1964 civil-rights legislation, the Gulf of Tonkin, Freedom Summer, Selma, Malcolm X, Watts, and the 1965 blackout**. This pass **read selected full-page newspaper OCR texts for the 46 new records HN-610–HN-655** as well as some rejected and contextual pages; **search-result snippets alone did not qualify a page for inclusion**. Selected ***Indianapolis Recorder*** pages offer crucial Black press coverage alongside syndicated stories from the ***Decatur Daily Democrat* and *The Daily Banner***. The selection is **not a representative sample of U.S. or international journalism**, nor a complete inventory of stories from these years. Comparison sources include the **National Archives, National Park Service, NASA, National Fire Protection Association, FDA, CDC, National Security Agency, U.S. Coast Guard, USGS, and the U.S. State Department**; their retrospective summaries are not necessarily complete original investigative case files. Key contradictions and chronology corrections include **King's 1956 conviction versus later bus integration (HN-610/HN-615)**; **the original report's uncertainty about Laika versus later recognition of her death shortly after launch (HN-619)**; **the school fire's initial 87 children versus 95 total deaths (HN-624)**; **the U-2 weather-plane cover story versus acknowledged reconnaissance (HN-630)**; **interim tsunami deaths and measured earthquake magnitude (HN-632)**; **Cuba's publicly described missile settlement versus private Turkey discussions (HN-640/HN-641)**; **Oswald charged but never tried (HN-645)**; **the reported Tonkin attack versus later doubts about August 4 (HN-648)**; and **separate March 1965 Selma confrontations that must not be collapsed into one photograph (HN-652)**. As before, attributed propaganda, preliminary death counts, allegations, and open investigations **do not become confirmed facts merely by appearing in print**.

23. **1966–1975:** Individually year-filtered search queries in the **Hoosier State Chronicles** index located original newspaper pages on **Gemini VIII, the Texas tower attack, China's Red Guards, Aberfan, Apollo 1, the Six-Day War, Detroit, the Silver Bridge, King's and Kennedy's assassinations, Prague, Mexico City, Apollo 8 and 11, Chappaquiddick, Hurricane Camille, Apollo 13, Kent and Jackson State, the Pentagon Papers, East Pakistan and Bangladesh, Attica, Munich, Watergate, Wounded Knee, Chile, the 1973 Arab–Israeli War and oil embargo, the 1974 tornadoes, Cyprus, Nixon and Ford, Cambodia, Saigon, and the Helsinki Final Act**. The **50 records HN-656–HN-705** are based on selected **full original-page OCR texts personally fetched and inspected**, including source pages with shifting death counts, contested law-enforcement accounts, eyewitness allegations, and community-press editorial reactions. Further candidate pages were searched but **were not converted into records without inspection**; several archive requests initially failed with **HTTP 520/502 or HTTP/2 errors** and were retried. This remains a **highly selective, nonrepresentative set of Indiana press holdings**, often reprinting international wire copy, **not an exhaustive 1966–1975 news survey**. Retrospective comparisons use **NASA, the University of Texas, Welsh archives and the Aberfan inquiry, the NTSB, Detroit Historical Society, New York State Archives, the National Archives, the U.S. State Department, NOAA, Ohio History Connection, and the U.S. Holocaust Memorial Museum**. Especially important cautions include **Aberfan's 107/“nearly 200” versus 144 confirmed dead (HN-659/HN-660)**; **the Silver Bridge's feared 80 versus 46 (HN-666)**; **Robert Kennedy wounded then confirmed dead on successive days (HN-668/HN-669)**; **the unproved Jackson State sniper assertion (HN-679)**; **Attica's reversal of the hostage-stabbing narrative (HN-683/HN-684)**; **alleged Chile executions (HN-694)**; **provisional 1974 tornado counts (HN-697/HN-698)**; and **March 1975 reporting that preceded Cambodia's atrocities and Saigon's final fall (HN-702–HN-704)**. Source OCR, including **1890 rendered as “1980” for Wounded Knee (HN-692)** and the Chile eyewitness figure, has not been fully checked against photographic originals. A surviving newspaper claim is **not a conviction, a verified death register, or proof of an alleged motive**.

24. **1976–1985:** Separate year-filtered searches of the **Hoosier State Chronicles** index surfaced pages about **Entebbe, Legionnaires' disease, Tenerife, New York's blackout, Jonestown, Skylab, the Iran hostage crisis, Mount St. Helens, the MGM Grand hotel fire, attempts on Reagan and Pope John Paul II, the Hyatt walkway collapse, Polish martial law, the Air Florida crash, Tylenol poisonings, Sabra and Shatila, Sally Ride, Korean Air Lines Flight 007, AIDS, Beirut, Grenada, Indira Gandhi's assassination and anti-Sikh violence, Ethiopia's famine, Bhopal, New Coke, Air India, the Mexico earthquake, and Nevado del Ruiz/Armero**. The **52 new records HN-706–HN-757** were selected from **full original-page OCR texts actually retrieved and read**, not search snippets; numerous other hits were rejected or left for later investigation. Archive index and OCR publication dates conflict on a few pages (particularly **Hyatt, HN-728**), and some searches failed with **HTTP 520** or HTTP/2 errors. The sample is **limited to digitized Indiana holdings**, which largely reprint national and international stories, and is **not an exhaustive investigation of the decade**. Retrospective cross-checks drew on **CDC, FBI, NASA, NTSB, NIST, EPA, USGS, the National Archives, U.S. State Department, U.S. Marine Corps, Royal Canadian Mounted Police, and contemporary newspaper accounts**. Corrections include **Tenerife 575/576 versus 583 (HN-710/HN-711)**; **first-day Skylab landfall denial (HN-719/HN-720)**; **Bhopal's incorrect “cyanide gas” versus methyl isocyanate (HN-747)**; **Air Florida's 77 versus 78 (HN-730)**; **Tylenol five to seven, with perpetrator unresolved (HN-733/HN-734)**; **Beirut 172 versus 241 (HN-740)**; and **Mexico's 230 to 2,000 and early 7.8 versus modern USGS M8.0 (HN-753/HN-754)**. The **1985 post-Armero practice drill** is **not another eruption or an advance evacuation (HN-757)**. Warnings, witness claims, attributed responsibility, and suspected criminal plots remain separate from completed inquiries and adjudicated facts. Paper image inspection and independent victim rosters are still needed for some particulars.

25. **1986–1995:** Individually year-scoped, topic-specific searches of **Hoosier State Chronicles** found newspaper leads for **Challenger, the Philippines' People Power transition, Chernobyl, Iran–Contra, AZT, USS Stark, Black Monday, Iran Air 655, Armenia, Lockerbie, Yellowstone, Exxon Valdez, Tiananmen, Polish democratization, Loma Prieta, the Berlin Wall, Mandela's release and election, Hubble, Iraq's invasion of Kuwait, the Gulf War, Pinatubo, the Soviet collapse, the Los Angeles riots, Andrew, Somalia, Bosnia, Waco, the World Trade Center bombing, the Mississippi floods, Rwanda, Northridge, Oklahoma City, the Chicago heat wave, and Dayton**. The **61 selected records HN-758–HN-818** are grounded in **original full-page OCR actually retrieved**, not merely snippets or archive counts. Some records are explicitly **eyewitness letters, local captions, or opinion pieces**, not official findings. Early broad searches sometimes failed with **HTTP 520/HTTP/2 errors**; narrower retries recovered many leads, while substantial archival gaps and unsampled topics remain. The source set is heavily **Indiana-based**, ranging from syndicated *Banner-Graphic* dispatches to the ***Indianapolis Recorder*, *Jewish Post*, and community newspapers**; it is **selective, nonrepresentative, and nowhere near an exhaustive decade census**. Comparisons use **NASA, UNSCEAR, FDA, SEC, U.S. Navy, NOAA, USGS, State Department, National Archives, FBI, CDC, Justice Department, South African government, Scottish prosecutors, and the U.S. Holocaust Memorial Museum**. Major corrections and cautions include **Chernobyl's initial two confirmed deaths versus later acute-radiation losses (HN-761)**; **USS Stark 28 versus 37 (HN-766)**; **Pan Am 103's 258/280 preliminary figures versus 270 victims (HN-774–HN-776)**; **Armenia's 50,000-plus estimate versus USGS's at-least-25,000 figure (HN-773)**; **Loma Prieta 271 versus 63 (HN-780/HN-781)**; **Iraq's unfulfilled August 1990 withdrawal promise (HN-787)**; **Pinatubo corn-weather speculation versus independently observed global cooling (HN-792)**; and **the distinction between Mandela's election and May 10 inauguration (HN-808)**. **Accusations of looting, sabotage, war crimes, fire-setting, criminal intent, and political conspiracy must not be silently treated as proved.** Further manual page-image checking, primary case-file research, and local/cross-national archive sampling remain necessary.

26. **1996–2005:** Individual year-filtered searches of **Hoosier State Chronicles** located period pages on **TWA Flight 800, ValuJet, Atlanta's Olympics and church arsons, Bosnia peacekeeping, Dolly's cloning, Hale–Bopp/Heaven's Gate, Hong Kong's handover, the Mars Pathfinder rover, tobacco records, Princess Diana, the India–Pakistan nuclear tests, East African embassy bombings, Swissair 111, Matthew Shepard, Hurricane Mitch, Columbine, Kosovo, East Timor, the İzmit earthquake, Y2K, Elián González, the contested 2000 election, USS Cole, September 11, anthrax, Afghanistan, corporate scandals, Iraq prewar claims, the Washington sniper attacks, Columbia, SARS, the 2003 blackout and European heat wave, early same-sex marriage licensing, Abu Ghraib, Madrid's false fingerprint identification, Beslan, the Indian Ocean tsunami, London transit bombings, Hurricane Katrina, Pakistan's earthquake, H5N1 fears, and Rosa Parks's death**. **The 75 new records HN-819–HN-893 are based on selected complete newspaper-page OCR fetched and reviewed**, not index snippets. Search terms produced uneven coverage: specific queries returned **no matching pages** even where synonyms found coverage, and year-by-year source availability differed. The sample is **highly selective, almost wholly digitized Indiana-area papers** (notably ***Indianapolis Recorder*, *Jewish Post*, *Muncie Times*, *Jefferson Booster*, and *Nappanee Advance-News***), often containing wire news, opinion, community notices, or student journalism. It is **not an exhaustive survey, an independent crime investigation, or a representative opinion sample**. Comparisons used **NTSB, NASA, FBI, Justice Department inspector general, National Archives, GAO, CDC, WHO, NOAA, USGS, U.S. State Department, Hong Kong government, California legislative history, European Court of Human Rights, the Library of Congress, and other institutional records**. Especially important corrections are **TWA 800's erroneous July 24 date and speculative bomb explanation versus the NTSB's July 17 fuel-tank finding (HN-820–HN-824)**; **Dolly as first cloned *adult-cell* mammal (HN-825)**; **an editor's own challenge to a six-million cult-member claim (HN-827)**; **East Timor's August 30 rather than August 31 referendum (HN-847)**; **election-night network projections versus certified returns (HN-850/HN-851)**; **anthrax investigation versus a never-tried suspect (HN-859/HN-860)**; **prewar Iraqi weapons claims versus later findings (HN-866/HN-870)**; **Columbia's early payload speculation versus the foam-damage investigation (HN-868)**; **the wrong Mayfield fingerprint match (HN-878)**; **Beslan's approximate 400 versus a later approximately 334 civilian deaths (HN-880)**; and **the tsunami's end-of-2004 >50,000 estimate versus February 2005 >220,000 (HN-881/HN-882)**. **Do not treat personal testimony as a population survey, commentary as established motive, preparatory action as completed implementation, or a preliminary estimate as a final verified toll.** Further page-image work and originating records would be needed to resolve remaining specifics.

27. **2006–2015:** Individually year-filtered and incident-focused searches in **Hoosier State Chronicles** found original newspaper pages about **Sago mine rescue misinformation, Katrina's continuing displacement and schools, Lebanon fighting, North Korea's nuclear test, a 2007 Indiana tornado and subsequent relief work, subprime-mortgage litigation and fraud, the 2008 financial crisis and presidential election, Zimbabwe cholera, H1N1, unemployment disparities, Haiti's earthquake, health-care reform, the Gulf oil spill and Ogoniland pollution, bin Laden's death, Occupy, the 2012 election and Sandy, the Paulsboro train derailment, Sandy Hook, Boston, the government shutdown, Typhoon Haiyan, Crimea, Ferguson, Ebola, ISIS, Charlie Hebdo, Indiana's RFRA, Syrian refugees and the Paris attacks**. **The selected original-page OCR for HN-894–HN-949 (56 records) was fetched and read**, with follow-up searches and corroboration against **MSHA, NWS, GAO, DOJ, NTSB, CDC, USGS, NOAA, WHO, UN/UNHCR/UNEP, the Federal Reserve and legislative or presidential records** as relevant. **It is not an exhaustive decade survey.** The collection's late holdings are concentrated in a few **Indiana local, Black-press and student newspapers**, and several major events had no usable page match; occasional **HTTP 520** errors required retries or left search coverage incomplete. **Keyword hits do not equal original pages read or independently confirmed events.** Particular corrections include **the false survival announcement at Sago (HN-894)**, **the purported North Korean 'nuclear missile' actually being an underground test (HN-900)**, **an F3/EF3 wording distinction for Nappanee (HN-904)**, **an indictment versus later verdict in the Jefferson case (HN-902)**, **Zimbabwe's early cholera toll versus WHO's later figures (HN-911)**, **H1N1's May 2009 initial 403 cases versus CDC's full-pandemic estimates (HN-913)**, **Haiti's mistaken '2009' date versus January 12, 2010 (HN-917)**, **the 200,000-gallon/day preliminary oil estimate versus NOAA's 134-million-gallon cumulative estimate (HN-920)**, **a leaked Niger Delta study's disputed framing versus the actual UNEP report (HN-921)**, **Sandy Hook's reported December 17 instead of December 14 date (HN-932)**, and **Ferguson's disputed hands-up claim, nonindictment and separate federal findings (HN-938/HN-942)**. Student editorials, paid-looking advocacy text, official statements, and actual investigation findings are marked as different source genres; **later corrections are not retroactive evidence of deception or concealment**.

28. **2016–2025:** Year-filtered searches of the **Hoosier State Chronicles** returned usable 2016–2023 articles from *Nappanee Advance-News* and Lafayette's student *Jefferson Booster*, but the available titles and topics became exceptionally narrow; targeted 2024–2025 event searches yielded **no usable results** in this collection. **Eleven selected Indiana full-page OCR texts were fetched and examined**. To maintain contemporary reporting through 2025 without pretending that the Indiana index represents national coverage, **50 additional original, dated articles in *The Guardian*** were retrieved for comparison, for **61 new records, HN-950–HN-1010**. The 2016–2025 selections represent **event- and keyword-directed examples, not a census of published newspapers**. Selected later documentation was checked from **EPA, NTSB, DOJ, USGS, and NWS**; many other outcomes still require original investigative records. The date used is the **original publication date**, where the online report may also display later modifications. Modern rolling news articles can change after first publication, so a current copy should not be assumed to preserve the exact wording visible on its first day. Important early-versus-later distinctions include **Orlando's 49 victims versus totals including the gunman (HN-952)**, **the Las Vegas student's incorrect October 2 shooting date (HN-959)**, **initial versus final Camp Fire fatalities (HN-965)**, **the October 2020 Great Barrington advocacy document versus verified science (HN-975)**, **Surfside missing-person counts versus fatalities (HN-979)**, **Uvalde's original police account versus the subsequent DOJ review (HN-986)**, **East Palestine's early wheel-bearing suspicion versus the NTSB's completed findings (HN-992)**, and **first-day 2024–2025 bridge, aviation and flood casualty reports versus later investigations (HN-997–HN-1010)**. Student opinions, witness accounts, initial news alerts, verified official findings, and untested predictions remain explicitly separated.
29. **2026 (January 1–October 22 only):** Targeted date- and subject-specific searches selected **46 contemporary press records HN-1011–HN-1056**, principally reports published by *The Guardian*, with one syndicated Associated Press report, another AP account of the Moon crater, and selected scientific or institutional cross-checks. Original article pages were opened where retrievable, including reports on Venezuelan and Iranian military developments, Minneapolis killings, Artemis II, Hungary's election, archaeological research, extreme weather, the World Cup, lunar and planetary observations, and October's still-developing news. This is **a nonexhaustive sample**, not an index of all 2026 newspapers, and the year **has not ended**. A few syndicated AP pages could not be opened directly; their accessible dated republications, searchable full-text extracts and accompanying institutional accounts support the limited descriptions. **Publication date, event date and later article-update time can differ**; some June and July stories crossed midnight between US and European or Asian editions. Especially consequential distinctions include **investigations versus contested first-day shooting allegations (HN-1012/HN-1015)**; **an Artemis launch delay, actual liftoff and subsequent crew return (HN-1016/HN-1023/HN-1024/HN-1026)**; **a provisional Iran ceasefire versus lasting peace (HN-1025/HN-1031)**; **Monte Verde's disputed re-dating hypothesis (HN-1022)**; **organic Martian compounds, a possible black-hole object and Enceladus simulations versus demonstrated extraterrestrial life (HN-1035/HN-1043/HN-1050)**; **a crater formed in 2024 but reported scientifically in 2026 (HN-1047)**; and **October threats, preliminary casualty counts, and interim court orders versus completed outcomes (HN-1052–HN-1056)**. All developments after October 22, 2026 remain for a later pass.

## Continuing research queue

- Expand full-text OCR inspection beyond the indexed page previews, especially entries missing from the first OCR window.
- Follow named people, vessels, courts, and places in subsequent newspaper issues to connect outcomes and corrections.
- Visually inspect names and figures that OCR could misread; give extra attention to HN-003, HN-008, and HN-018.
- Revisit the missing 1795 index batches after the archive's rate limit lifts.
- Return to the unsampled majority of 1796–1805 issues, including later months of each year, after the archive's limit lifts; make another pass at 1800, for which no new item was selected from this restricted sample.
- Return to the vast unsampled portion of 1806–1815, including U.S. newspaper titles beyond the Indiana collections and the other dates of *The Statesman*; revisit LOC once rate limits clear.
- Return to unsampled 1816–1825 issues and titles, including reprinted reports whose names or figures should be checked against original local papers and foreign official records.
- Expand 1826–1835 across additional newspapers and regions; make a focused **1828** search (no entry was selected from that publication year in this sample) and revisit 1830–1835 stories whose original pages reprint claims from distant papers.
- Check 1833 claims about the Preston execution and the *Freedom* steamboat case against British court sources and Mississippi-region proceedings; neither is verified beyond the period's newspaper accounts here.
- Expand 1836–1845 beyond this keyword-selected Indiana archive sample, including other cities' original newspapers, the 1837 Swartwout inquest records, the 1838 bank prosecutions, and the fate of the *Braganza* defendants.
- Read the complete 1842–1843 *Somers* inquiry and later trial materials alongside the competing editorials; **do not treat one printed editorial or summary as a final resolution**.
- Trace the full texts and subsequent corrections of the 1844 “Roorback” and “Garland” political forgeries, and compare the competing 1845 New York fire explosion theories with later engineering and investigation records.
